The guide of Ask Gitardo, made by cargo xtask guide from the cards of docs/features: do not edit it.
@places desktop
Bar of views: The column of icons on the left edge: Timeline, Changes, Files, Compare, Activity, Settings.
Side column: The island on the left, the whole height of the window: **Repositories** above, **Branches** under it.
Repositories: The first section of the side column: a row, a **tab**, for each repository open, and **+**.
Header: The bar across the top of the window: repository, worktree and branch, Search, Sync, the menu of the view, **…**.
Island: Each rounded card of the window: the side column, the timeline, the inspector, a page.
Status bar: The line of text at the foot of the window: what is running, what was said, the identity commits are made with.
Branch rail: **Branches**, the second section of the side column: local branches, a heading for each remote, and **Kept aside** (stashes, worktrees, tags, submodules).
Timeline: The history with its lanes, in the middle. "The graph" is the drawing of its lanes.
Draft: The card of the uncommitted changes at the top of the timeline.
Inspector: The card of the selected commit, on the right.
Changes page: Every uncommitted file, staged or not, with its diff; opened by **Review** in the draft.
Files page: The tree of the working tree, each file that differs from the last commit marked, where a file or a folder is picked for its history, its blame or a comparison.
Search page: Files, folders and lines of text found in the worktree shown, as it is typed, with the preview of the one under the keyboard; opened by **Search in files** in the bar of views, `Ctrl+Shift+F`, **Search in files…** of Quick search and **Search here** in the context menu of the Files page.
Commit, Compare, Diff, Merge, Conflicts, File, Settings, Welcome and Worktree setup pages: The other pages, each opened in place of the timeline with its trail.
Bottom panel: The island under the timeline: the terminals, **Activity** (what is running and the Tasks) and the Command log.
Quick search: `Ctrl+K`: commits, branches, files and commands.
@places extension
Graph, in the panel: the toolbar, the references, the graph and the details. Also as an editor tab (Gitardo: Open Graph in Editor).
Gitardo Repositories, Commits, Gitardo Graph, Branches, Stashes and Gitardo Changes, in the Source Control side bar, under VS Code's own views and folded at first. Gitardo has no icon of its own in the activity bar.
Tabs of the editor: a commit, a comparison, the review of the resolutions of conflicts, what new worktrees get, the Flow Diff and the Flow Merge.
The editor itself: the blame of the line under the cursor, the blame of the whole file beside its lines, and the buttons on the titles of the diff and merge editors that switch to the Flow Diff and the Flow Merge.
The Explorer and the Source Control view of VS Code: entries in their context menus (history of a file or folder, compare with, stash this file, link into new worktrees).
The Command Palette: 82 commands, all named Gitardo: …, listed in `package.json` under `contributes.commands`.
The Chat of VS Code: the participant `@gitardo`, which answers how to do something with Gitardo, with a button under the answer for each feature it names that has a command (Gitardo: Ask How To… opens it).
The Gitardo output channel: the log of every command run.
The walkthrough *Get started with Gitardo* (`media/walkthrough/`), which VS Code opens once after installing.
@card desktop repo.read-repositories
title: Find the repositories of a workspace
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
The repositories a window can show: the one each folder of the workspace is in, the ones directly inside a folder, and the submodules of all of these that are checked out.
@card desktop repo.name-repositories
title: Name the repositories in lists
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
How a repository is called on its tab (`repo.tabs`), in the repository menu of the extension, in the picker and in the activity indicator.
@card desktop repo.open-folder
title: Open a folder
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Shows a folder, or several, in the window: replaces what the window shows, or adds to it.
Entry points: **Open folder…** on the Welcome page, in the menu of **+** at the end of the tabs, in the repository menu of the extension, and in Quick search.
@card desktop repo.open-recent
title: Open a recent workspace
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Opens again a workspace the window showed before, with the tabs it had and the repository that was shown in it.
Entry points: the list of recent workspaces on the Welcome page; **Open recent** in Quick search.
Refuses: a workspace whose folders are all gone: "None of the folders of this workspace is there any more." The workspace is taken out of the list.
@card desktop repo.clone
title: Clone a repository
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Copies a repository from its URL, or from its folder on disk, into a new folder, and opens the copy in a tab.
Entry points: **Clone a repository…** on the Welcome page, beside **Open folder…**; in the menu of **+** in the heading of **Repositories**, after **Open folder…**; on the timeline of `repo.no-repository`; **Clone Repository…** in Quick search. The window has no menu bar, and the title bar it draws has none (`window.title-bar`): the menu of **+** is where the window opens what it shows.
Refuses: what the boxes refuse while typed; a folder that has files by the time it runs: **`<full path>` is not empty.**
@card desktop repo.folder-from-url
title: The name of a clone's folder
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
The name the folder of a clone gets by default: the name of the repository, from its URL or its path.
@card desktop repo.init
title: Create a repository
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Makes an empty repository in a folder, with the first branch git's configuration says or the one typed, and opens it in a tab.
Entry points: **Create a repository…** on the Welcome page and in the menu of **+** in the heading of **Repositories**, after **Clone a repository…**; **Create Repository…** in Quick search; **Create a repository here** on the timeline of `repo.no-repository`, for the folder of the workspace that has none (with several, the first;).
Refuses: a folder that is the top of a repository already, where `git init` would only initialize it again: **`<folder>` is a repository already.**
@card desktop repo.select
title: Choose the repository shown
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Gitardo shows one repository at a time; in a workspace with several, this chooses which.
Entry points: the tabs (`repo.switch-tab`), and the repository menu of the header in the extension; **Select repository…** in the **…** menu of the header; **Select Repository** in Quick search. In Gitardo Desktop, choosing a repository of a tab shows that tab, as `repo.switch-tab` does.
Offered when: the workspace has more than one repository, or the window shows a worktree of a repository of the workspace that is not in the workspace itself (see `worktree.switch`). The repository menu of the extension lists the repositories to choose from only then.
Refuses: - while an operation runs: nothing happens; in Gitardo Desktop the tab is shown all the same, as `repo.switch-tab` says; - from Quick search, with a single repository: "Gitardo: this workspace has a single repository."; with none: "Gitardo: no Git repository in this workspace."
@card desktop repo.owner-of-file
title: The repository a file is in
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
When a file is chosen for its history or its blame, the window switches on its own to the repository that has it.
@card desktop repo.no-repository
title: No repository in the workspace
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
What the window shows when the workspace has no repository, or the one it had cannot be read.
Entry points: the timeline and the branch rail, in place of what they list.
@card desktop repo.read-state
title: Read the state of the repository
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
Everything the window shows of a repository but its commits: what is checked out, the branches and how far each is from its upstream, the remotes, the tags, the stashes, the worktrees, the submodules, what is not committed, and the operation that stopped half way.
@card desktop repo.read-shallow
title: Whether the history is shallow, and where it ends
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
Whether the repository is a shallow clone, and the commits whose parents were not fetched, where its history ends.
@card desktop repo.common-folder
title: The git folder the worktrees share
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
The git folder every worktree of a repository shares, where its references live: the `.git` of the main worktree, whichever worktree is shown. The extension watches it for the changes of the references.
@card desktop repo.read-git-version
title: Read the version of git
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
Which git runs the operations, so that a feature that needs a newer one says what it does without it, rather than failing in git's words.
@card desktop repo.watch-changes
title: Which changes ask for a read
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
The repository is read again on its own when it or its files change, from Gitardo, a terminal or another program. This says which changes count, and how soon.
@card desktop repo.schedule-reads
title: Read again when something changed
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
Decides when the repository is read: once after a burst of changes, never twice at once, again while something keeps changing during each read, never during an operation. **Refresh** asks for a read at once.
Entry points: **Refresh**, the first entry of the **…** menu of the header; **Refresh** in Quick search; and every change `repo.watch-changes` counts.
@card desktop activity.describe-task
title: Say what Gitardo is doing
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
The words the activity indicator of the status bar and the **Activity** tab of the bottom panel use for each thing that takes time.
@card desktop activity.status-bar
title: What is running, in the status bar
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
The status bar at the foot of the window says what Gitardo is running, for how long, and why, what the last operation said (`activity.said`), and the identity commits are made with.
Entry points: the status bar.
@card desktop activity.said
title: What was said, in the status bar
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
What an operation said when it ended, and the notices of the window, in the status bar, with **Undo** and what it offers next, in place of the bars that were under the header.
Entry points: the status bar, after what is running.
@card desktop activity.what-is-running
title: What is running, in the Activity tab
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
Lists what Gitardo is doing now, in a tab of the bottom panel, beside the commands it runs.
Entry points: the tab **Activity** of the bottom panel (`panel.bottom`); a click on the activity indicator of the status bar; **Activity** in the bar of views; **Show What Is Running** in Quick search.
@card desktop welcome.page
title: The Welcome page
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: page
On the first start, a page that shows in five steps where the timeline, the branch rail and the inspector are and what to try first, and opens a folder.
Entry points: shown by itself the first time Gitardo Desktop starts; **Welcome** in Quick search and in the Help menu; the window with no workspace.
@card desktop repo.tabs
title: The repositories open, in the side column
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
**Repositories**, the first section of the side column: a row for each repository open, the rows of its worktrees and its submodules under it, the one shown marked, what each is on, and **+** to open another.
Entry points: **Repositories** at the top of the side column (`repo.side-column`). `Ctrl+1` to `Ctrl+8` show the repository at that place and `Ctrl+9` the last one; `Ctrl+PageDown` and `Ctrl+PageUp` the next and the one before, going round. On macOS `Cmd+1` to `Cmd+9`, `Cmd+Shift+]` and `Cmd+Shift+[`.
Offered when: a workspace is open, even with a single repository, so that **+** is there.
@card desktop repo.side-column
title: The side column of repositories and branches
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
One island on the left of the window, the whole height of it, with **Repositories** above, **Changes** under it and **Branches** last, each folding by its heading, the lines between them dragged.
Entry points: the left of the window, beside the bar of views, under the header; it is put away and brought back by the first button of the top row of the timeline and of the title row of every page (`graph.toggle-references`).
@card desktop repo.worktree-summary
title: What a repository or a worktree is on
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
The branch checked out in a repository or a worktree, how many files are changed there, and how many commits are to pull and to push, for the rows of **Repositories** and the rows of the worktrees and the submodules under them.
@card desktop repo.open-tab
title: Open a repository in a tab
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Opens a repository of the workspace, or of a folder added to it, in a tab of its own.
Entry points: **+** in the heading of **Repositories**, whose menu lists the repositories of the workspace that are not open, then **Open folder…**, **Clone a repository…** (`repo.clone`), **Create a repository…** (`repo.init`) and **Open workspace file…**; **Open Repository in a Tab** in Quick search.
@card desktop repo.choose-tabs
title: Choose the repositories a workspace opens
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: show where
When a workspace with several repositories is opened for the first time, says which of them open in tabs: all of them, or the ones the user keeps checked.
Entry points: `repo.open-folder` and `repo.open-code-workspace`, once the repositories of the folders opened are found; **Open folder…** in the menu of **+**.
Offered when: the folders opened hold more than one repository that would have a tab of its own (`repo.group-tabs`: worktrees and submodules do not count), and the workspace was not opened before. A workspace opened before opens the tabs it had (`repo.open-recent`).
Refuses: nothing checked, or `Escape`: nothing opens, and the window stays as it was.
@card desktop repo.open-code-workspace
title: Open a workspace file of VS Code
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Opens the folders a `.code-workspace` file of VS Code lists, so that the window shows the repositories of a workspace the user already has in VS Code.
Entry points: **Open workspace file…** on the Welcome page, in the menu of **+** and in Quick search.
Refuses: - a file that names no folders: "This file names no folders."; - a file that cannot be read, or is no JSON: what is wrong with it; - a file whose folders are all gone: "None of the folders of this workspace is there any more."
@card desktop repo.close-tab
title: Close a tab
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Takes a repository out of the window. Nothing in the repository changes.
Entry points: the **×** of its row in **Repositories**; a middle click on the row; **Close tab** and **Close other tabs** in its context menu; **Close Tab** in Quick search, for the tab shown.
Refuses: a tab where an operation runs: "Gitardo: wait for `<title>` to end before closing this tab."
@card desktop repo.switch-tab
title: Show another tab
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Shows the repository of another tab, as it was left.
Entry points: a click on its row in **Repositories**, and the keys of `repo.tabs`; **Go to Repository…** in Quick search, which lists the tabs; and whatever shows a repository of another tab: a file of it chosen for its history or its blame (`repo.owner-of-file`).
Offered when: the window has two tabs or more.
Refuses: nothing; showing the tab shown already reads nothing.
@card desktop repo.keep-open
title: Keep what each repository showed
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
What a repository keeps while another is shown, so that coming back to it finds it as it was, at once.
@card desktop repo.group-tabs
title: Worktrees and submodules in the tab of their repository
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
Which repositories share a tab: a repository, its worktrees and its submodules are one tab, whose row in **Repositories** has theirs under it, and a click on one of those shows it in the tab.
Entry points: the rows under the row of a repository in **Repositories**, folded and unfolded by the chevron at its left: its other worktrees, each by the name of its folder, then its submodules, each by its path, a submodule's own submodules indented further; each is one line, as the row of a repository is, and says what it is on on its right, `feature/login ● 3 ↓2 ↑1` (`repo.worktree-summary`), ending where the rows of the repositories end. The one the tab shows is marked as the row shown is. A click on one shows it in the tab, as `worktree.show-in-gitardo` and `submodule.show-in-gitardo` do. The worktree in the header lists the repository, its worktrees and its submodules, the same choice (`graph.where`).
@card desktop repo.watch-hidden
title: Keep the tabs not shown up to date
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
How the repositories of the tabs not shown follow their changes without reading at every one of them.
@card desktop trust.is-trusted
title: Whether a folder is trusted
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
@card desktop trust.ask
title: Ask whether to trust a folder before running what it asks
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
When an operation would run what a folder asks and the folder is not trusted, Gitardo asks first, so that a repository just cloned runs nothing from its `.gitardo/settings.json` before the user has read it.
Offered when: the folder of the repository is not trusted (`trust.is-trusted`) and there is something to run; otherwise it asks nothing and gives the trust the window has.
@card desktop trust.manage
title: See the trusted folders and stop trusting some
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Entry points: **Trusted folders…** in Quick search.
Refuses: with no folder trusted, **No folder is trusted.**
@card desktop upkeep.read-size
title: The size of a repository
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
How much room the objects of a repository take, loose and packed, and how much of it git could put away.
@card desktop upkeep.clean-up
title: Clean up a repository
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: quick search
Packs the loose objects of a repository and deletes those nothing has reached for a long time, with `git gc`, saying the room it takes before and after.
Entry points: **Clean up…** in the context menu of the row of a repository in **Repositories** (`repo.tabs`), on the row shown alone, since an operation runs on the repository the views show; Quick search, **Clean Up the Repository…**, is not there yet.
Offered when: no operation of Gitardo runs in the repository.
Refuses: git's message when another `git gc` runs in the repository.
@card desktop upkeep.read-background
title: Whether git looks after a repository in the background
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
Whether the repository is among those git's scheduled maintenance looks after.
@card desktop upkeep.start-background
title: Have git look after a repository in the background
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: show where
Registers the repository for git's scheduled maintenance, which fetches in the background into hidden references, packs and checks the repository each hour, day and week, so that it stays quick without a clean-up by hand.
Entry points: **Look after it in the background…** in the context menu of the row of a repository in **Repositories**, unticked.
Offered when: the repository is not registered (`upkeep.read-background`).
Refuses: git's message when the system's scheduler refuses, as without the rights to it.
@card desktop upkeep.stop-background
title: Stop having git look after a repository
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: show where
Takes the repository out of those git's scheduled maintenance looks after; the others stay as they are.
Entry points: **Look after it in the background**, ticked, in the context menu of the row of a repository in **Repositories**.
Offered when: the repository is registered.
@card desktop upkeep.read-commit-graph
title: Whether git keeps a commit-graph file
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
Whether the repository has git's commit-graph file, the cache of its commits and their parents that lets git list the history without walking all of it first, and whether git reads it.
@card desktop upkeep.write-commit-graph
title: Speed up reading the history
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: show where
Writes git's commit-graph file for the repository, with the paths each commit changed, so that the history, a search and the history of a file are read about twice as fast.
Entry points: **Write it** in the notice of `upkeep.offer-commit-graph`; **Speed up reading the history** in the context menu of the row of the repository shown in **Repositories**, after **Clean up…**.
Offered when: the repository has no commit-graph file and git reads one (`upkeep.read-commit-graph`), and no operation of Gitardo runs in it.
Refuses: git's message, as when another git writes the file at the same time.
@card desktop upkeep.offer-commit-graph
title: Offer the commit-graph file
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
A notice, once, for a repository with a long history and no commit-graph file, that git can read it about twice as fast with one.
Entry points: the notices of the status bar (`activity.status-bar`), when the read of the whole history of the repository shown ends.
@card desktop graph.read-commits
title: Read the commits of the graph
place: the timeline
show: none
The commits the timeline lists, newest first, with their parents, a page at a time as git prints them: one read goes on to the end of the history, or to 100,000 commits.
@card desktop graph.read-position
title: Where a commit is in the history listed
place: the timeline
show: none
How many commits come before a commit in the history the timeline lists, to know how far it must be loaded for the commit to be there; or that it is not in it.
@card desktop graph.view
title: The timeline
place: the timeline
show: none
The commits of every branch, remote branch and tag with their lines and labels, the branch rail beside them and the inspector of the selected commit: what the window shows while no page is open, and the first step of every trail.
Entry points: the window, while no page is open; **Timeline**, the first step of the trail of a page; **Open Timeline** in Quick search shows it.
@card desktop graph.toolbar
title: The header
place: the timeline
show: none
Where one is and what applies to the whole repository, in a bar across the top of the window, always shown.
Entry points: the top of the window.
@card desktop graph.where
title: Where one is, in the header
place: the timeline
show: none
The repository, the worktree and the branch shown, at the start of the header, which is a bar across the top of the window, always shown.
Entry points: the start of the header (`graph.toolbar`), above whatever is shown.
@card desktop window.brand
title: The logo and the name of Gitardo
place: the timeline
show: none
The logo of Gitardo at the top left of the window, and its name at the start of the header bar.
Entry points: always shown, above the bar of views and at the start of the header bar.
@card desktop window.title-bar
title: The header bar as the title bar of the window
place: the timeline
show: none
The window has no title bar of the system: the free parts of the header bar move it, maximize and restore it, and open the system's menu of the window, and its edges resize it.
Entry points: the pointer on the header bar or on the edges of the window; `Alt+Space`.
@card desktop window.caption-buttons
title: Minimize, maximize and close
place: the timeline
show: none
**Minimize**, **Maximize** or **Restore**, and **Close**, at the right end of the header bar on Windows and Linux, drawn by Gitardo.
Entry points: the three buttons at the right end of the header bar, after **…**.
@card desktop graph.search-button
title: The button of Quick search
place: the timeline
show: none
**Search** in the header opens Quick search, as `Ctrl+K` does.
Entry points: **Search** in the header; `Ctrl+K` (`Cmd+K` on macOS) anywhere in the window.
@card desktop graph.islands
title: The islands of the window
place: the timeline
show: none
The side column, the timeline, the inspector and the page shown are each a rounded card on the ground of the window, under the header.
Entry points: the whole window.
@card desktop graph.view-bar
title: The bar of views
place: the timeline
show: none
A column of icons on the left edge of the window, each the way to a place of it.
Entry points: the left edge of the window.
@card desktop panel.bottom
title: The bottom panel
place: the timeline
show: none
An island under the timeline and the inspector, or the page shown, with the terminals, the tasks of the repository, what is running and the Command log, each in a tab of its own.
Entry points: `` Ctrl+` `` Cmd+` `` on macOS), which shows the panel at its terminal, or hides it when it has the focus; **Terminal** in the bar of views (`graph.view-bar`); **Show Terminal** and **Show Command Log** in Quick search; the activity indicator of the status bar, **Activity** in the bar of views and **Show What Is Running**, which show it at **Activity** (`activity.what-is-running`).
@card desktop terminal.view
title: A terminal in the bottom panel
place: the timeline
show: none
A shell in the folder of the repository shown, inside the window, with the colors and the keys of a terminal.
Entry points: **Terminal** in the bottom panel (`panel.bottom`), which starts one when there is none; **New terminal** there; `` Ctrl+Shift+` ``; **New Terminal** in Quick search.`
@card desktop graph.icons
title: The icons of the window
place: the timeline
show: none
Every icon of the window is one of Lucide's, each with one meaning, so that the same action looks the same everywhere, and as it does in other applications; the logo of Gitardo is not, and is drawn the same way (`window.brand`), nor are the icons beside the names of files and folders, which are those of Symbols (`file-view.file-icons`).
Entry points: every button, item of a menu (`ui.menu-icons`), row and mark of the window that has an icon.
@card desktop graph.view-menu
title: The menu of the view of the header
place: the timeline
show: none
Every switch of what the timeline shows and how it draws it, in one menu apart from the actions.
Entry points: the button **View**, with the icon of the sliders, right before **…** at the end of the header.
Offered when: a repository is read; before, the button cannot be clicked.
@card desktop graph.more-menu
title: The … menu of the header
place: the timeline
show: none
The actions on the repository and the ways to what is not elsewhere in the header, apart from the switches of the timeline, which are in the menu of the view (`graph.view-menu`).
Entry points: **…** at the end of the header.
@card desktop ui.menu-scroll
title: A menu taller than the room under it
place: the timeline
show: none
A menu, or a level of one, that does not fit the window scrolls inside it, so that every entry can be reached, and it stays where it opened.
Entry points: every menu of the window: the menus under a button (the menu of the view, **…**, **Sync**, the repository and the worktree of the header), the context menus and their submenus.
@card desktop ui.menu-icons
title: The icon of each action in the menus
place: the timeline
show: none
Every item of a menu that does something shows the icon of its action at the left of its words, the one a button of the same action draws, so that the eye finds an action in a long menu and one meaning is one icon across the window.
Entry points: every context menu and every menu under a button: the menu of a commit and of its labels in the timeline, of the draft, of a stash, of a selection, of a drop; the menus of a branch, a remote branch, a tag, a worktree, a stash and a submodule in the side column, and of their lists; the menu of the view, the **…** and the **Sync** menus of the header, the repository and the worktree of the header, the menu of a repository tab and the **+** of **Repositories**; the menus of a file in the Files page, the inspector, the Commit page and the comparison; the menu of a file of the changes and the **…** of their heading (`changes.row-menu`); the menus of the editors of the File, Diff and Merge pages; the chooser of the comparison, the sections of the settings.
@card desktop ui.picker-scroll
title: A picker longer than its rows
place: the timeline
show: none
The list of a picker scrolls with a bar as well as the wheel, and keeps the row lit in view, so that a long list can be gone through with the pointer or the keys.
Entry points: every picker: Quick search, the filters of the timeline and of the branch rail, the choices of an operation.
@card desktop ui.page-trail
title: The way back, in the title of a page
place: the timeline
show: none
The title row of a page names the pages it was reached through, each a way back to that page as it was left, rather than always `Timeline › <page>`.
Entry points: the title row of every page, after the button of the side column (`repo.side-column`); `Alt+Left`.
@card desktop graph.load-more
title: Load the next commits while scrolling
place: the timeline
show: none
The commits come a page at a time as they are read, without waiting for the list to be scrolled, so that scrolling does not stop to wait for them; past the 100,000 commits of one read, the next read starts long before the end of those shown.
Entry points: scrolling the list of commits.

title: Where the history of a shallow clone ends
place: the timeline
show: none
In a shallow clone the timeline says where the commits fetched end, and offers to fetch more, so that the last commit listed is not taken for the first commit of the project.
Entry points: the timeline and the inspector of a shallow repository.

@card desktop search.go-to-hash
title: Go to a commit by its hash
place: the header, Search and Quick search
show: none
Type the hash of a commit, or its first four characters or more, with **Loaded** chosen; if it is not among the loaded commits, `Enter` asks git for it wherever it is.
Entry points: the search box, with **Loaded** chosen.
Offered when: the text, its spaces taken away, is 4 to 64 hexadecimal digits in any case, and no loaded commit matches it. A commit among the loaded ones is highlighted like any other match instead (`search.loaded`).
Refuses: none or several commits start with it: "Gitardo: "abcd" is not a commit of this repository, or is the start of several.", with the text typed in place of `abcd`.

Puts the whole message of a commit on the clipboard, as it was written.
Entry points: **Copy message** in the context menu of a commit in the timeline, narrowed or not.
@card desktop commit-page.open
title: Open a commit as a page
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: quick search
Opens a commit whole, in a page of its own, or shows the page it has.
Entry points: double-click on a commit in the timeline, narrowed or not, or in its narrow layout, or `Enter` on the commit selected; the button beside the one that closes the inspector; **Open as a page** in the context menu of a commit; a parent in the page of a commit, a side, a commit or the merge base in the comparison page; Quick search: **Open Commit as a Page…**.
Offered when: on a commit or a stash; not on the draft. A double click on the label of a branch checks the branch out instead (`branch.checkout`).
Refuses: a hash that is not a commit of the repository: **`<short hash>` is not a commit of this repository.**

@card desktop changes.discard
title: Discard the changes of a file
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Takes one file back to the last commit, staged changes included, or deletes it when it is untracked; several files given at once are discarded together.
Entry points: **Discard changes** in the menu of a file of the Changes page (`changes.row-menu`).
Offered when: the file is changed for git.
Refuses: "There is nothing to discard." when git sees no change in the file any more.
@card desktop changes.discard-all
title: Discard all changes
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Takes every changed file back to the last commit, staged changes included, and deletes the untracked files.
Entry points: **Discard all changes** on the header of the list in the Changes page; **Discard all changes…** in the context menu of the draft in the timeline.
Offered when: in the context menu of the row, while no merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert or `git am` is in progress: there, **Abort** is what goes back. alone: - Nothing staged: `git restore -- <path>…` for the tracked files and `git clean -f -- <path>…` for the untracked ones, each file named. - Something staged: `git restore --source=HEAD --staged --worktree -- <path>…` with every file of the list, renames with both names, and `git clean -f -- <path>…` for the untracked files not staged. - Before the first commit, the staged files are deleted with `git rm -r --force --quiet -- <path>…`.
@card desktop changes.save

place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Writes to disk the edits typed in Gitardo's editors and not saved yet, so that git sees them.
Entry points: **Save all** on the header of the list, for every file of the working tree with edits not saved in a File page. **Save** in the menu of a row of the Changes page (`changes.row-menu`) saves that file through `file-view.save`, which asks before writing over a file another program changed.
Offered when: the file has edits not saved (`changes.unsaved`).

@card desktop changes.ignore
title: Ignore a file or a folder
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Has git ignore a file or a folder, by a pattern written where the user chooses; on a file git tracks, offers to stop tracking it too, since a pattern does not stop git from following a file it tracks.
Entry points: **Ignore…** in the menu of a file of the changes (`changes.row-menu`), in the Changes page and in the **Changes** section; **Ignore…** in the context menu of a file or a folder of the Files page.
Offered when: the file or the folder is in the repository, outside its submodules, and no pattern ignores it. Not on a file in conflict, nor on a file only an editor has changed.
Refuses: a path that a pattern ignores already: **`<path>` is ignored already, by `<pattern>` in `<file>`.**; a path in a submodule: **`<path>` is in the submodule `<name>`: ignore it there.**
@card desktop changes.stop-tracking
title: Stop tracking a file, and keep it on disk
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Takes a file, or every file of a folder, out of the index, so that the next commit deletes it from the repository while it stays on disk: for a file committed by mistake, such as a file of local settings, once it is ignored.
Entry points: the third question of `changes.ignore`; **Stop tracking, keep on disk…** in the menu of a tracked file of the changes (`changes.row-menu`) and in the context menu of a file or a folder git tracks in the Files page.
Offered when: git tracks the file, or files under the folder; not on a file in conflict. The Files page offers it on every folder, since it does not know which folders hold tracked files; on a folder with none it refuses.
Refuses: **Git does not track `<path>`.**; a file in conflict: **`<path>` is in conflict: resolve it first.**

Which tracked files git is told not to look at, and how: those marked **skip-worktree**, whose changes stay out of commits and which a checkout leaves alone, and those marked **assume unchanged**, a promise git may forget.
@card desktop changes.skip

place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Has git leave the changes of a tracked file alone: they stay on disk and out of every commit, as for a settings file that each one changes on their own computer.

Offered when: git tracks the file, it is not hidden already, and it is not in conflict. Not on a folder.
Refuses: on a file git does not track: **Git does not track `<path>`: ignore it instead.** with **Ignore…** (`changes.ignore`).

@card desktop branch.create-orphan

@card desktop branch.validate-name

@card desktop branch.rename

@card desktop branch.delete
title: Delete a branch
place: the branch rail
show: show where
Deletes a local branch, after saying whether its commits are merged, so that none is lost without knowing.
Entry points: **Delete** in the context menu of a local branch.
Offered when: the branch is not checked out here, and no other worktree has it checked out; there, **Remove worktree and delete branch…** does both (`worktree.remove`).
@card desktop branch.clean-up
title: Clean up branches
place: the branch rail
show: quick search
Lists the local branches that are done with, merged or left without their upstream, and deletes the ones ticked, with the worktrees that have them.
Entry points: **Clean up branches** in Quick search; **Clean up branches…** in the **…** menu of the header.
Refuses: when no branch is merged or has lost its upstream, before asking anything: "No local branches are merged or have lost their upstream."; when the pick is accepted with nothing ticked: "No branches selected."
@card desktop branch.fast-forward
title: Fast-forward a branch that is not checked out
place: the branch rail
show: show where
Brings a local branch up to its upstream without switching to it: the files and the branch checked out are not touched.
Entry points: **Fast-forward to `<upstream>`** in the context menu of a local branch.
Offered when: the branch is not checked out here; it has an upstream that is not gone; it has no commits of its own as of the last fetch; and, when another worktree has it checked out, that worktree's folder is there and it has no file not committed. It is offered however far behind the last fetch says it is: the fetch finds out.
Refuses: when the branch no longer meets the conditions above: "`<branch>` cannot be fast-forwarded: it needs an upstream, no commits of its own, and not to be checked out here or in a worktree with changes not committed."
@card desktop branch.set-upstream
title: Set or change the upstream of a branch
place: the branch rail
show: quick search
Makes a local branch follow a remote branch, or another one than it follows: the one it pulls from, pushes to and is counted ahead of or behind.
Entry points: **Set upstream…** in the context menu of a local branch with no upstream, **Change upstream…** in that of one with an upstream, gone or not, in the branch rail and on its label in the timeline; **Set upstream of the current branch…** in Quick search.
Offered when: the repository has a remote branch.
Refuses: when the remote branch picked is no longer there: "`<remote branch>` is no longer there: fetch, then pick again."
@card desktop branch.unset-upstream
title: Stop tracking the upstream
place: the branch rail
show: show where
Makes a local branch follow nothing: it is no longer counted against a remote branch, and the remote branch stays.

Offered when: the branch has an upstream.

Brings what the upstream has into the branch checked out, the way the settings choose.

Pulls in one of the four ways, whatever the settings say **Pull** does.

@card desktop sync.push
title: Push
place: the branch rail
show: quick search
Sends the commits of the branch checked out to its upstream, or, for its first push, to the remote, making that its upstream.
Entry points: **Push** in the menu of **Sync** in the header, which has the number of commits to push when there are some, and whose tooltip is **Push: git push**; **Push** in Quick search. **Sync** pushes the same way after its pull (`sync.sync`).
Refuses: on a detached `HEAD`: "Cannot push: HEAD is detached. Check out a branch first."; in a repository with no remote: "This repository has no remote." with **Add remote…** (`remote.add`).
@card desktop sync.sync
title: Sync with the upstream
place: the branch rail
show: quick search
Fetches, pulls what the upstream has, then pushes what the branch checked out has ahead of it: in one click, what the counts on **Sync** promise.
Entry points: a click on **Sync** in the header (`graph.toolbar`), which shows beforehand the commits to pull and to push as of the last fetch, `↓2 ↑1`, and whose tooltip is **Sync: git fetch, git pull, git push**; **Sync** in Quick search. **Fetch**, **Pull** and its other ways, **Push** and its other ways stay in the menu of **Sync**.
Refuses: what a pull and a push refuse: on a detached `HEAD`, "Cannot sync: HEAD is detached. Check out a branch first."; in a repository with no remote, "This repository has no remote." with **Add remote…** (`remote.add`); with `pull.rebase` on and `pull.autostash` off, while a tracked file has changes, staged or not, which would stop the pull: "Commit or stash the changes first: the pull cannot rebase over them."

The commits of the upstream that the branch checked out does not have, as of the last fetch: what a force push throws away.

@card desktop selection.cherry-pick
title: Cherry-pick several commits
place: the timeline
show: show where
Applies several selected commits onto what is checked out, with one `git cherry-pick`, so that git treats them as one operation to continue, skip or abort.
Entry points: **Cherry-pick onto `<current branch>`** in the selection bar; **Cherry-pick N commits onto `<current branch>`** in the context menu of the selection.
Offered when: two commits or more are selected, none of them is a merge, and no merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert, patch or bisect is under way.
Refuses: while an operation is under way, *Finish or abort the `<operation>` first.*; when fewer than two of the selected commits are loaded, *Select the commits to cherry-pick in the timeline.*; with a merge among them, *A merge commit can only be cherry-picked on its own.*
@card desktop cherry-pick.commit
title: Cherry-pick a commit
place: the timeline
show: show where
Applies the changes of one commit onto what is checked out, as a new commit, after saying which files will conflict.
Entry points: **Cherry-pick `<short hash>`** in the context menu of a commit; a commit dropped on the commit checked out (`drag.drop`); **Cherry-pick** in the menu of a reflog entry (`reflog.browse`).
Offered when: the commit is not the one checked out.
@card desktop cherry-pick.skip-empty
title: Get past a commit that changes nothing
place: the timeline
show: show where
When a cherry-pick or a revert stops on a commit whose changes are already there, or whose conflicts were resolved by keeping what `HEAD` has, git will not continue until that commit is skipped, and says so in words that read like a failure. Gitardo skips it, as a rebase would, and says so in the log.
Entry points: none of its own: it is the follow-up on failure of `cherry-pick.commit`, `selection.cherry-pick`, and of `conflicts.continue-operation` and `conflicts.skip-step` during a cherry-pick or a revert.
Offered when: the step that failed was a `git cherry-pick` or `git revert` command.
Refuses: never; it does nothing when the conditions do not hold.
@card desktop revert.commit

Makes a new commit that takes back the changes of a commit.
Entry points: **Revert `<short hash>`** in the context menu of a commit in the timeline, narrowed or not.
Offered when: on every commit, the one checked out included.
@card desktop reset.to-commit
title: Reset the current branch to a commit
place: the timeline
show: show where
Moves the branch checked out (or `HEAD` when detached) to a commit, keeping the changes staged, unstaged, or not at all.

@card desktop merge.read-predicted-conflicts

Works out, without touching the index or the working tree, which files a merge would leave in conflict.

@card desktop merge.into-current
title: Merge into the current branch
place: the timeline
show: show where
Merges a commit, branch or remote branch into what is checked out, as the configuration of git says: a fast-forward when one is possible, otherwise a merge commit.
Entry points: **Merge into `<current branch>`** in the context menu of a commit, a local branch and a remote branch; **Merge `<branch>` into `<current branch>`** in the menu of a branch dropped on the commit checked out (`drag.drop`).
Offered when: something is checked out (the repository has a commit), and the commit or the branch's tip is not the commit checked out; for a local branch, it is not the one checked out.
@card desktop merge.choose-way
title: Merge into the current branch, choosing how
place: the timeline
show: show where
The entry after **Merge into `<current branch>`** asks which of the four ways to merge, as the menu beside **Pull** has the ways to pull.
Entry points: **Merge into `<current branch>`…** (with the hint *choose how*) wherever `merge.into-current` is offered, and **Merge `<branch>` into `<current branch>`…** in the menu of a branch dropped on the commit checked out.
Offered when: as `merge.into-current`.
Refuses: the fast-forward only when it is not possible, without running git: when `<ref>` has nothing `<current>` has not, *`<current>` already has every commit of `<ref>`.*; otherwise *`<current>` cannot be fast-forwarded to `<ref>`: it has commits that `<ref>` has not. Merge it another way, or rebase `<current>` onto it.*
@card desktop merge.undo-squash
title: Take back a squash merge
place: the timeline
show: show where
A squash merge stages changes and moves no branch, so the reflog cannot take it back: this takes what it staged out of the index and the working tree.
Entry points: **Undo** in the **…** menu of the header right after a squash merge (`undo.last` finds it in the journal); **Undo** in the message that follows the squash merge.
Offered when: the journal's entry for the squash merge still holds: the top of the reflog of `HEAD` is what it was when the squash merge ran (nothing committed or moved since). Also after a squash merge that stopped on conflicts.
@card desktop rebase.onto
title: Rebase the current branch onto a commit or a branch
place: the timeline
show: show where
Makes the commits of the branch checked out again on top of another commit, branch or remote branch.
Entry points: **Rebase `<current branch>` onto this** in the context menu of a commit, a local branch and a remote branch; **Rebase `<current branch>` onto `<branch>`** in the menu of a branch dropped on the commit checked out; **Rebase `<current branch>` onto `<short hash>`** in the menu of the current branch dropped on another commit (`drag.drop`).
Offered when: as `merge.into-current`: something is checked out and the target is not the commit checked out.
Refuses: nothing of its own; when git refuses (changes not committed), git's words are shown.
@card desktop rebase.branch-onto
title: Rebase another local branch onto a commit
place: the timeline
show: show where
Rebases a local branch that is not checked out onto the commit it is dropped on; git checks it out first.
Entry points: **Rebase `<branch>` onto `<short hash>`** in the menu of a local branch, not checked out, dropped on another commit (`drag.drop`).
Offered when: the branch is local, is not at the commit it is dropped on, and no other worktree has it checked out.
Refuses: *`<branch>` is checked out: rebase it onto `<short>` instead.*; *`<branch>` is checked out in another worktree: rebase it there.*
@card desktop rebase.find-line-before-head
title: The line of commits an interactive rebase can rewrite
place: the timeline
show: none
The commits that an interactive rebase starting at a commit would make again: from `HEAD` down to that commit, when they form a single line without merges. It decides where the interactive rebase, **Edit message…**, **Split this commit…**, **Squash…** and the fixup are offered.
@card desktop rebase.read-pushed
title: Where a commit was pushed
place: the timeline
show: none
The remote branches that have a commit, read before a commit is rewritten, so that the confirmation can say the branch will have to be force pushed.
Entry points: none: used by `message.edit`, `squash.commits`, `squash.into-previous` and `split.commit`.
@card desktop rebase.read-stacked-branches

place: the timeline
show: none
The local branches, other than the one rewritten, whose tip is one of the commits a rebase is about to make again: the branches of a stack, each started from the one under it, which the rebase would leave on the old commits.
Entry points: none: read by the operations of `rebase.update-refs` before they make their plan.
@card desktop rebase.update-refs
title: Which branches move with a rewrite
place: the timeline
show: none
Decides whether a rewrite takes the other branches of a stack with it, and says which in its plan, so that a branch on a commit made again is not left on the old one.
@card desktop rebase.todo-page
title: The interactive rebase page
place: the timeline

The list of the commits from a commit up to `HEAD`, to reorder by dragging and to pick, reword, edit, squash, fixup or drop each one before the rebase starts.
Entry points: **Interactive rebase of N commits…** in the context menu of a commit, or **Edit this commit (interactive rebase)…** when that commit is the one checked out.
Offered when: `rebase.find-line-before-head` gives a line for the commit, and no merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert, patch or bisect is under way.
@card desktop rebase.build-todo-list
title: The todo list of an interactive rebase
place: the timeline

Turns the rows of the interactive rebase page, or the steps another operation prepares, into the todo list git reads, and the files of the new messages.
@card desktop rebase.interactive
title: Run an interactive rebase
place: the timeline
show: show where
Runs `git rebase -i` with the todo list prepared on the interactive rebase page, by having git's editor of the todo list copy the prepared one over it, since no editor can be typed into.
Entry points: **Start rebase** on the interactive rebase page (`rebase.todo-page`). The same plan is what `message.edit`, `squash.commits`, `squash.into-previous` and `split.commit` build for an older commit.
Offered when: as `rebase.todo-page`.
@card desktop rebase.read-stop

Tells a stop to edit a commit, or to split one, from a stop on a conflict, and names the commit.

@card desktop rebase.edit-stop

While an interactive rebase is stopped right after a commit marked **Edit**, the banner says so and what to do.

@card desktop message.edit
title: Edit a commit message
place: the timeline
show: show where
Gives a commit a new message: the last one by amending it, an older one by an interactive rebase that makes the commits after it again.
Entry points: the pencil beside the message in the inspector (tooltip *Edit message*); **Edit message…** in the context menu of a commit.
Offered when: the commit is not the draft; no merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert, patch or bisect is under way; and the commit is the one checked out (merge or not) or `rebase.find-line-before-head` gives a line for it.
Refuses: *Finish or abort the `<operation>` first.*; *End the bisect first.*; an empty message, *Enter a message.*; an older commit with no line to `HEAD`, *`<short hash>` is not on the line of commits before HEAD without merges: its message cannot be changed by a rebase from here.*
@card desktop squash.join-messages
title: The message of commits squashed into one
place: the timeline
show: none
Puts together the message of the commit that several become.
@card desktop squash.commits
title: Squash commits into one
place: the timeline
show: show where
Makes one commit of several that follow one another before `HEAD`, with the changes of them all.
Entry points: **Squash into one** in the selection bar; **Squash N commits into one…** in the context menu of the selection; `gitardo-cli run squash.commits commits:<hash>,<hash>…`. Its target is the commits selected.
Offered when: two commits or more are selected; the oldest of them has a line to `HEAD` (`rebase.find-line-before-head`) and the others are the commits right after it on that line, with none missing; and no merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert, patch or bisect is under way.
Refuses: *Finish or abort the `<operation>` first.*; *End the bisect first.*; when they do not follow one another on that line, *Only commits that follow one another on the line before HEAD, without merges, can be squashed into one.*
@card desktop squash.into-previous
title: Squash a commit into the commit before it
place: the timeline

The same as squashing two commits, from the context menu of the newer one.
Entry points: **Squash into the commit before it…** in the context menu of a commit.
Offered when: the commit has a line to `HEAD`, it has one parent, and that parent has a line to `HEAD` too; no operation or bisect is under way.
@card desktop split.commit
title: Split a commit
place: the timeline
show: show where
Takes the changes of a commit out of it into the working tree, unstaged, to commit them again in as many commits as wanted.
Entry points: **Split this commit…** in the context menu of a commit.
Offered when: where `message.edit` is, and the commit has exactly one parent (not a merge, not the first commit).
Refuses: *Finish or abort the `<operation>` first.*; *End the bisect first.*; a hash that is not a commit; a merge, *`<short>` is a merge: it cannot be split.*; the first commit, *`<short>` is the first commit, with nothing before it to take its changes back to: it cannot be split.*; an older commit with no line to `HEAD`, *`<short>` is not on the line of commits before HEAD without merges: it cannot be split by a rebase from here.*
@card desktop split.continue
title: Continue a split
place: the timeline
show: show where
What **Continue** of the banner does while a rebase is stopped to split a commit: go on once its changes are committed, or commit what is left of them as one more commit.
Entry points: **Continue** in the banner while `rebase.read-stop` gives a split (`conflicts.continue-operation` hands over to this).
Offered when: a rebase is stopped to split a commit.
Refuses: when other files have changes not committed, *`<file>` has changes that are not committed and are not part of the split: commit or stash them, then continue.* (or *N files have changes…*); **Go back to committing** runs nothing.
@card desktop fixup.commit
title: Add changes to an earlier commit
place: the timeline
show: show where
Folds the staged changes, or all the changes when nothing is staged, into an earlier commit, and makes the commits after it again.
Entry points: **Add staged changes to this commit (fixup)**, or **Add all changes to this commit (fixup)** when nothing is staged, in the context menu of a commit.
Offered when: `rebase.find-line-before-head` gives a line for the commit; it is not the one checked out (**Amend** covers that); there are staged changes, or changes not staged; no file is in conflict; and no operation or bisect is under way.
Refuses: *Finish or abort the `<operation>` first.*; *End the bisect first.*; with no change, *There are no changes to add to that commit.*

@card desktop range-diff.read-pairs

show: none
The commits of two lines paired, as `git range-diff` pairs them: the same commit made again, a commit changed with what changed in it, a commit added, a commit dropped.
@card desktop range-diff.compare
title: See what a rewrite changed
place: the timeline
show: show where
Opens, commit by commit, what a rebase or an amend changed in a branch, what a force push would replace, or what someone else's force push replaced.
Entry points: - **See what changed** in what is said after a rebase, an interactive rebase and the operations built on it, an amend and a pull that rebased, once they ended and made at least one commit again; - **What its last rewrite changed** in the context menu of a local branch; - **Compare with `<upstream>` commit by commit** in the context menu of a local branch that has commits its upstream has not and the other way round, as of the last fetch, and among the other ways to push in the menu of **Sync** while the branch checked out is so; - **Compare first** in the warning of `sync.push-force-with-lease` and of `sync.push-force`, which closes it, runs nothing and opens the page; - after a fetch that found the upstream of a local branch force pushed, "`<remote branch>` was force pushed." with **See what changed**; and **What the force push changed** in the context menu of that remote branch.
Refuses: "`<branch>` has not been rebased or amended lately." when its reflog has no rewrite; "`<branch>` and `<upstream>` are at the same commit." when there is nothing to compare.
@card desktop range-diff.page
title: What a rewrite changed, commit by commit
place: the timeline
show: page
The commits of a branch before and after a rewrite, paired: the ones made again unchanged, the ones changed with what changed in each, the ones added and the ones dropped.
Entry points: `range-diff.compare`.
@card desktop stash.read-list
title: Read the stashes
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
The stashes of the repository, newest first, as the branch rail and the timeline list them.
@card desktop stash.read-files
title: Read what a stash changes
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
The files a stash changes, and the untracked files it took along, for what applying it would stop on.
Refuses: a name that is not of the form `stash@{N}`: **"`<name>`" is not a stash.**
@card desktop stash.pane
title: The Stashes list
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
Lists the stashes, in **Kept aside** of the branch rail; each unfolds to the files it holds, which open as diffs.
Entry points: the **Stashes** list in **Kept aside** of the branch rail; Quick search: **Show Stashes**, which opens the list and shows it, with the branch rail and its filter box emptied; **Show Stashes** in the message of `stash.autostash-left`.
@card desktop stash.push
title: Stash all changes
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: quick search
Sets every change aside, staged or not, untracked files included, and leaves the working tree as the last commit.
Entry points: **Stash** in the **…** menu of the header (**Stash: git stash push --include-untracked**); the button on the title of the Stashes list; **Stash all changes…** on the header of the list in the Changes page; **Stash changes…** in the context menu of the draft; **Stash all changes…** above the files of the draft in the inspector when none is ticked; Quick search: **Stash Changes**.
Offered when: there is a commit (git has nothing to stash onto before the first one); **Stash** in the header also needs changes, and is disabled while Gitardo is running an operation.
@card desktop stash.push-staged
title: Stash the staged changes
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Sets aside only what is staged; the rest stays where it is.
Entry points: **Stash only the staged changes…** on the header of the list in the Changes page; **Stash staged changes…** in the context menu of the draft.
Offered when: there is a commit and something is staged.
Refuses: git refuses `--staged` before Git 2.35, and the failure is shown with git's words.
@card desktop stash.push-file
title: Stash a file, or the files ticked
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Sets aside every change to one file, or to the files chosen, staged or not.
Entry points: **Stash this file…** in the menu of a file of the Changes page (`changes.row-menu`), and on the row of a file in the inspector while the draft is selected; **Stash N selected files…** above the files of the draft in the inspector, while some are ticked.
Offered when: there is a commit; not on a file whose changes are only unsaved edits in Gitardo's editor.
@card desktop stash.preview
title: What applying a stash would stop on
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
Says, before a stash is applied or popped, what git would stop on: the conflicts its changes make with what is committed, the changed files it would refuse to touch, and the untracked files it took that are there again.
@card desktop stash.apply
title: Apply a stash
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Brings the changes of a stash back into the files, and keeps the stash.
Entry points: **Apply** on the row and in the context menu of a stash in the Stashes list of the branch rail; in the context menu of its row in the timeline.
@card desktop stash.pop
title: Pop a stash
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Brings the changes of a stash back into the files, and drops the stash once they are in without conflicts.
Entry points: **Pop** on the row and in the context menu of a stash in the Stashes list of the branch rail; in the context menu of its row in the timeline.
@card desktop stash.drop
title: Drop a stash
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Deletes a stash and the changes it holds.
Entry points: **Drop…** on the row and **Drop** in the context menu of a stash in the Stashes list of the branch rail; **Drop** in the context menu of its row in the timeline.
@card desktop stash.undo-drop
title: Undo a stash dropped
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Brings back a stash dropped in Gitardo, while its commit is still in the repository.
Entry points: **Undo** in the **…** menu of the header (`undo.last`), right after `stash.drop`.
Offered when: the last operation Gitardo ran was the drop, and the last entry of the reflog of `HEAD` is the one there was when it ran: nothing else happened since. Otherwise Undo takes back what the reflog says.
Refuses: git refuses when the commit of the stash has been collected since; its words are shown.
@card desktop stash.branch
title: Create a branch from a stash
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Makes a branch at the commit a stash was made on, checks it out, and applies the stash there, where it cannot conflict with what came after.
Entry points: **Create branch from stash…** in the context menu of a stash in the Stashes list of the branch rail and of its row in the timeline.
Refuses: git refuses when the files it would check out have changes; its words are shown.
@card desktop stash.in-graph
title: Stashes in the timeline
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
Shows each stash in the timeline, above the commit it was made on and where its date puts it, so that what was set aside is seen where it belongs.
Entry points: the timeline and its narrow layout; **Show stashes** in the menu of the view of the header, which switches them on and off.
@card desktop stash.autostash-left
title: The stash a pull leaves
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
A pull with `--autostash` sets the changes aside, pulls, and puts them back; when they no longer fit on what came in, git leaves them in conflict in the files and keeps the stash. Nothing says so unless git's output is read: Gitardo says it, and offers the stashes.
Entry points: after `sync.pull` with `--autostash`, **Pull** or another way to pull in the menu of **Sync**.
@card desktop conflicts.banner
title: The banner of an operation that stopped
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Says that an operation is waiting for the user, how many files are in conflict, and gives the ways on, back and to the resolutions, at the top of the timeline and of the Changes page.
Entry points: shown by itself over the commits of the timeline and over the list of the Changes page.
Offered when: a merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert or `git am` is in progress, or at least one file is in conflict without one (as after a stash that did not apply cleanly). Otherwise it is not drawn.
@card desktop conflicts.continue-operation
title: Continue
place: the Conflicts page
show: quick search
Goes on with the operation that stopped, once its conflicts are resolved and staged.
Entry points: **Continue** in `conflicts.banner`; **Continue** in the message of `conflicts.resolve-automatically`; **Continue the merge** (or rebase, cherry-pick, revert, am) in the report of `review.write`; **Continue the operation** in Quick search.
Offered when: an operation is in progress and no file is in conflict.
Refuses: while files are in conflict: *Resolve the conflicts and stage the files first.*
@card desktop conflicts.skip-step
title: Skip
place: the Conflicts page
show: quick search
Leaves out the commit or patch the operation stopped on and goes on with the next one.
Entry points: **Skip** in `conflicts.banner`; **Skip this step** in Quick search.
Offered when: a rebase, cherry-pick, revert or `git am` is in progress, and the rebase did not stop to edit or split a commit (skipping there would throw away what is not committed and keep the commit anyway).
Refuses: during a merge: *A merge has no steps to skip.*
@card desktop conflicts.abort-operation
title: Abort
place: the Conflicts page
show: quick search
Takes the repository back to how it was before the operation started.
Entry points: **Abort** in `conflicts.banner`; **Abort the operation** in Quick search.
Offered when: a merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert or `git am` is in progress.
@card desktop conflicts.stopped
title: The message when an operation stops on conflicts
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Says that an operation stopped on conflicts, what to do next, and offers the resolutions at once.
Entry points: shown as the failure follow-up of any operation that leaves files in conflict (merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert, `git am`, applying or popping a stash, a fixup).
Offered when: the operation failed and at least one file is in conflict afterwards. Any other failure shows the first `error:` or `fatal:` line of git with **Show log**.
@card desktop conflicts.read-files
title: The files in conflict and their sides
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Which files are in conflict, and which of the three versions git keeps in the index each of them has.
@card desktop conflicts.side-names
title: What the two sides are
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
The words that say what **Current** and **Incoming** are in the operation that stopped, the branch or commit each one is, and the subject of its commit. The Merge page, the review page, the questions to the AI provider and the questions of `conflicts.take-side` all say the sides this way.
@card desktop conflicts.open-file
title: Open a file in conflict
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Opens a file in conflict where it can be resolved by hand, which depends on what the two sides did to it.
Entry points: a click on a file in the **Conflicts** group of the Changes page; a click on a file in conflict in the inspector, while the draft is selected in the timeline; the line number of a card in `review.page`.
Offered when: the file is in conflict (`conflicts.read-files`); a file no longer in conflict is not opened.
@card desktop conflicts.mark-resolved
title: Mark as resolved
place: the Conflicts page
show: show where
Tells git that a file's conflicts are resolved, by staging it.
Entry points: **Mark as resolved** in the menu of a file of the **Conflicts** group of the Changes page (`changes.row-menu`).
Offered when: the file is in conflict.
@card desktop conflicts.take-side
title: Take the current side, take the incoming side
place: the Conflicts page
show: show where
Resolves a whole file for one side when that side is simply right: the file becomes what that side has, or is deleted when that side deleted it.
Entry points: **Take the current side for the whole file** and **Take the incoming side for the whole file** in the menu of a file in conflict in the Changes page (`changes.row-menu`); **Keep it, with these changes** and **Delete it** on a card of `review.deleted-card`.
Offered when: the file is in conflict.
Refuses: *\<file\> is not in conflict.* when it has no stages.
@card desktop conflicts.parse-markers
title: The conflicts git marked in a file
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Finds the conflicts in the text of a file as git marked them, in any of git's styles (`merge`, `diff3`, `zdiff3`), and whatever names git wrote after the markers.
@card desktop conflicts.resolve-by-rules
title: The conflicts that need no choice
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Resolves the conflicts of a file that need no choice between the two sides, and says why each one could be resolved. Git stops wherever the two sides changed lines that touch; many of those stops are two changes side by side, the same change twice, or the same lines spaced differently. Every line it writes is a line of one of the two sides, as it is in the file: it never makes one up. Anything that is not plainly one of its cases is left exactly as git marked it.
@card desktop conflicts.resolve-automatically
title: Resolve conflicts automatically
place: the Conflicts page
show: quick search
Writes the resolutions of `conflicts.resolve-by-rules` in the files at once, after saying what they are, with a way to look at each one first.
Entry points: **Resolve conflicts automatically** in Quick search; **Resolve automatically** in `conflicts.stopped`. (The buttons of the banner and of a file open `review.page` instead.)
Offered when: `conflicts.autoResolve` is on (it is marked **Experimental** in the settings) and files are in conflict.
Refuses: with `conflicts.autoResolve` off: *Resolving conflicts automatically is experimental, and it is switched off in the settings.* with **Open settings**. With no marked conflict: *There are no conflicts marked in the files to resolve.* With none resolvable: *None of the 3 conflicts can be resolved without choosing a side.* When a file changed between the question and the writing: *src/app.rs has changed since it was read, so nothing was written. Try again.*, and no file at all is written.
@card desktop conflicts.restore
title: Undo a resolution
place: the Conflicts page
show: show where
Marks the conflicts of files again as git had marked them when it stopped, after Gitardo wrote resolutions in them or took one side of them.
Entry points: **Undo** in the **…** menu of the header right after `conflicts.resolve-automatically`, `review.write` or `conflicts.take-side`; **Undo** in the message that follows them and in the report of `review.write`.
Offered when: the last thing Gitardo did in this repository was one of those, and the reflog has not moved since.
@card desktop review.page
title: The review of the resolutions
place: the Conflicts page
show: page
A page with every conflict of the files in conflict, one card each, where what is proposed for each one is looked at, accepted or not, and written. Nothing is written until the user writes what was accepted.
Entry points: **Review the resolutions of the conflicts** in Quick search; **Resolve … automatically** and **Resolve with AI** in `conflicts.banner`; the wand (**Resolve its conflicts automatically, after looking at what is proposed**) and the sparkle (**Resolve with AI, after looking at what is proposed**) in the menu of a file in conflict in the Changes page (`changes.row-menu`), which open it with that file alone; **Review** in the question of `conflicts.resolve-automatically`.
Offered when: `conflicts.autoResolve` or `conflicts.ai` is on.
Refuses: with both settings off: *Resolving conflicts automatically is experimental, and it is switched off in the settings.* with **Open settings**.
@card desktop review.file-list
title: The files and the filters
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
The list of the files in conflict beside the cards, with where each one stands, and the filters that keep both the list and the page to some of the conflicts.
Entry points: the left part of `review.page`.
@card desktop review.card
title: A conflict, as a card
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
One conflict: what is proposed first, with where each of its lines comes from and what it does with the two sides, what looks wrong in it, the other ways to resolve it, what each side changed on demand, and where the proposal comes from beside the buttons that decide.
Entry points: one card per conflict in `review.page`, under its file.
@card desktop review.side-diff

What one side of a conflict changed of the base, as a diff shows it, numbered as in the file.
@card desktop review.fingerprint

show: none
A key that is the same for the same conflict as long as nobody edits it, wherever it is in the file, whatever is edited around it, and however git named the sides after the markers (which changes when `conflicts.restore` puts them back).
@card desktop review.proposals

Which proposal a conflict has, if any, and the lines of the file it takes the place of.
@card desktop review.proposal-lines

Tells, for each line of a proposal, which side has it, and what the proposal does with the two sides as a whole, so that a card can say it in a sentence and in colors.
@card desktop review.proposal-warnings

place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Finds in a proposal what is most likely a mistake of keeping both sides: a setting or a variable given a value twice, and a JSON file that would no longer be JSON.
@card desktop review.choose
title: Choose a side, or write the lines
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Puts in place of what is proposed for a conflict one of its sides, both one after the other, or lines written in the card, accepted at once, without leaving the review.
Entry points: **Current only**, **Incoming only**, **Both, current first**, **Both, incoming first** and **Edit** under what is proposed (after **Instead:**, or **Choose:** with nothing proposed); **Drop your choice** on a choice.
Offered when: the language model is not writing about the conflict, and nothing runs.
@card desktop review.accept

Takes the proposal of one conflict, to be written with the others.
Entry points: **Accept** on a card; **Accepted** on the same card takes it back.

@card desktop review.accept-file

@card desktop review.accept-all

Takes every proposal of the page, from the rules, from the AI and chosen, but those with a warning.

@card desktop review.clear

Offered when: something is accepted.
@card desktop review.write

place: the Conflicts page
show: show where
Writes the accepted resolutions in the files as one operation, and stages the files left with no conflict.
Entry points: **Write N resolutions** (*Write the accepted resolutions in the files, and stage the files left with no conflict*) in the head of `review.page`.
Offered when: at least one conflict is accepted; otherwise the button reads **Write the accepted** and is disabled.
Refuses: *No resolution is accepted.*; *src/app.rs has changed since it was read, so nothing was written. Try again.*, and then the page reads the files again.
@card desktop review.report

Says what the last write did, in place of the buttons, with the way back and the way on.

@card desktop review.columns

Shows each conflict in three columns, as the Merge page does: the current side, what is proposed, and the incoming side.

@card desktop review.deleted-card

@card desktop review.recorded

@card desktop ai.resolve-conflicts

Asks the AI provider how to resolve each conflict the rules leave, one conflict per question, and shows each answer as a proposal to accept or not.
Entry points: **Resolve with AI** in `conflicts.banner` and in `conflicts.stopped`; the sparkle in the menu of a file in conflict in the Changes page (`changes.row-menu`); **Resolve conflicts with AI** in Quick search; in `review.page`, **Ask the AI about the rest (N)** (*Ask the language model about each conflict that has nothing proposed*) and **Ask the AI** or **Ask again** on a card.
Offered when: `conflicts.ai` is on (it is marked **Experimental** in the settings).

@card desktop ai.ask-conflict
title: One question about one conflict
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Sends the AI provider the question about one conflict and returns its answer as it comes.
Refuses: an answer that stops at the most tokens the model may write (`stop_reason` `max_tokens`, or `finish_reason` `length` for `openai-compatible`), with *The answer of the model was cut at its length. Ask again with a lower effort.*, rather than read a resolution cut in half.

@card desktop ai.trim-answer

Small models often write the lines around the conflict again with their answer. Where those lines are as they were sent, only what stands in place of the conflict is kept.
@card desktop ai.choose-model
title: Choose the model that resolves conflicts
place: the Conflicts page
show: quick search
Chooses which model of the AI provider is asked about conflicts, and how much it thinks before it answers.
Entry points: **Choose the model that resolves conflicts** in Quick search; the button with the model's name in the head of `review.page` (*Choose the language model that resolves the conflicts, and how much it thinks*), which reads **No language model** when the provider has none.
Refuses: when the provider lists no model: *No language model is available from the AI provider. Set one up in the settings.*; with GitHub Copilot signed out, *GitHub Copilot is not signed in.* with **Sign in**; without its CLI, *The GitHub Copilot CLI is not installed. Install it, then try again.* with **How to install**.
@card desktop ai.copilot
title: GitHub Copilot as the AI provider
place: the Conflicts page
show: none
Asks GitHub Copilot, with the user's own sign-in and subscription, the questions Gitardo asks a language model: the conflicts the rules leave, and the draft of a commit message.
Refuses: without `copilot` on the `PATH`: *The GitHub Copilot CLI is not installed. Install it, then try again.* with **How to install**, which opens GitHub's page on installing it; when it is not signed in: *GitHub Copilot is not signed in.* with **Sign in**, which opens a new terminal of the bottom panel and types `copilot login` in it; when the CLI speaks a protocol older than the SDK's: *The GitHub Copilot CLI is too old for Gitardo. Update it, then try again.* with **How to install**, since installing it again updates it; when newer: *The GitHub Copilot CLI is too new for Gitardo. Update Gitardo, then try again.*; when Copilot refuses the question (no subscription, no requests left, a model not offered): *GitHub Copilot refused: \<its words\>*. A draft or a conflict refused so says it in the status bar with its button; the refusal of `ai.choose-model` offers it as its button.
@card desktop rerere.read-state

Whether git records and writes again the resolutions of conflicts in this repository, and whether it also stages what it writes.
@card desktop rerere.turn-on
