Turns on, for this repository, git's recording of the resolutions of conflicts and their use the next time.
Entry points: **Remember how conflicts are resolved…** in the context menu of the row of a repository in **Repositories** (`repo.tabs`), unticked; Quick search, **Remember How Conflicts Are Resolved…**.

@card desktop rerere.turn-off

Turns git's recording of resolutions off for this repository; what it recorded stays, unused.
Entry points: **Remember how conflicts are resolved**, ticked, in the context menu of the row of a repository in **Repositories**; Quick search, **Stop Remembering How Conflicts Are Resolved**.
Offered when: it is on.
@card desktop rerere.read-resolved

@card desktop rerere.read-marked

@card desktop rerere.resolved-as-before

place: the Conflicts page

Says, wherever the files in conflict are listed, which of them git resolved from a recorded resolution, and gives what can be done with each: look at what git wrote, mark it resolved, or forget the resolution.
Entry points: drawn in `conflicts.banner`, in the message of `conflicts.stopped`, on the rows of the files in conflict of the changes, and in `review.page` (`review.recorded`).
@card desktop rerere.forget
title: Forget a resolution git recorded
place: the Conflicts page
show: show where
Has git forget the resolution it recorded for the conflicts of a file, and marks them again, for a resolution that was wrong and that git would otherwise write each time.
Entry points: **Forget this resolution…** in the menu of a file resolved as before (`rerere.resolved-as-before`) and **Forget it** on its card in the review (`review.recorded`).
Offered when: the file was resolved as before during the operation under way, staged or not.
@card desktop compare.resolve-revision
title: Which commit a revision is
place: the Compare page
show: none
Turns what the user types or chooses, a hash, a branch, a tag, `HEAD~3`, `v1.0^` or `main@{yesterday}`, into the commit it stands for, or says there is none.
@card desktop compare.side-choices

@card desktop compare.recent

@card desktop compare.choose
title: Compare branches, tags or commits
place: the Compare page
show: quick search
Asks for the two sides of a comparison in the comparison chooser (`compare.chooser`), and opens it.
Entry points: **Compare Branches, Tags or Commits…** in Quick search; **Compare** at the top of the branch rail; **Compare branches, tags or commits…** in the **…** menu of the header.
Refuses: a text accepted before its check came back, which git does not know: **"`<text>`" is not a commit of this repository.**
@card desktop compare.chooser
title: The comparison chooser
place: the Compare page
show: none
One dialog to choose both sides of a comparison, each seen while the other is chosen, in place of two questions one after the other.
Entry points: `compare.choose`, and `compare.change-side` with the side clicked chosen.
@card desktop compare.follow-sides
title: Followed sides and fixed ones
place: the Compare page
show: none
A side named by a branch, a tag or a revision follows it as it moves; a side given by its hash stays that commit.
@card desktop compare.initial-mode
title: How two sides are compared at first
place: the Compare page
show: none
Two branches or tags are compared as a pull request shows them, a commit or the working tree directly.
@card desktop compare.read-sides
title: Read a comparison
place: the Compare page
show: none
Everything the comparison page shows of two sides, except the lines of the files.
Refuses: two sides that are both the working tree, or that are not commits of the repository: **These are not commits of this repository.**
@card desktop compare.read-patch
title: Read the lines of a comparison
place: the Compare page
show: none
What changed in each file of a comparison, for its page, and the same as a patch to copy.
@card desktop compare.page
title: The comparison page
place: the Compare page
show: page
Two sides compared on a page: how far apart they are, what merging would do, the commits only in each and the files that differ, with their changes.
Entry points: `compare.choose`, `compare.with`, `compare.path-with`, **Open as a page** of `compare.in-place`, an entry of **Opened before**.
@card desktop compare.beside-diff
title: A comparison beside its diffs
place: the Compare page
show: none
A file of the comparison page opens its diff on the left, and the comparison stays on the right as a column, so that the files of a comparison are read one after the other as those of a commit are.
Entry points: a click on a file of the comparison page; **Open in the Diff page** of the comparison page; `Alt+Down` and `Alt+Up` in the Diff page opened so, for the next and the previous file of the comparison.
@card desktop compare.summary
title: How far apart, and what merging would do
place: the Compare page
show: none
The sentences under the sides of a comparison.
@card desktop compare.change-side
title: Choose another side
place: the Compare page
show: show where
Puts another branch, tag, commit or the working tree in place of one side, in the same page.
Entry points: a click on either side at the top of the comparison page, or of its column beside a diff (`compare.beside-diff`).
@card desktop compare.swap
title: Swap the two sides
place: the Compare page
show: none
Compares in the other direction: what the second has against the first becomes what the first has against the second.
Entry points: the arrows between the two sides of the comparison page (**Compare in the other direction**).
@card desktop compare.set-mode
title: Since they diverged, or everything that differs
place: the Compare page
show: none
Chooses which changes are listed: what the second changed since it left the first, as a pull request shows it, or all that differs between the two, what the first changed meanwhile included.
Entry points: the two buttons of the comparison page: **Since they diverged** (`git diff <first>...<second>`) and **Everything that differs** (`git diff <first> <second>`), whose tooltips say these commands with the names of the sides.
Offered when: **Since they diverged** is disabled, with the tooltip **Their histories never meet**, when the sides have no merge base.
@card desktop compare.narrow-to-path

place: the Compare page

Shows only the files of a file or a folder, and only the commits that change it.
Entry points: the box under the summary of the comparison page (**Only the changes of this file or folder**); `compare.path-with`, which opens a comparison narrowed already.
@card desktop compare.commits

show: none
Lists the commits that tell the two sides apart: the ones only in the second, then the ones only in the first.
Entry points: **Commits** in the comparison page, shown when either side has commits of its own.
@card desktop compare.take-file
title: Take the version of the second side
place: the Compare page
show: show where
Makes a file in the working tree and the index what the second side of the comparison has: its version of the file, or no file when it has none.
Entry points: the arrow on the row of a file in the comparison page: **Take the version of `<second>` into the working tree**, or, for a file the second side deleted, **Delete it from the working tree, as `<second>` has no such file**.
Offered when: the second side is not the working tree.
@card desktop compare.copy-patch

Copies the changes of the comparison, as listed, as a patch that `git apply` takes, binary files included.

@card desktop compare.open-on-github
title: Open the comparison on GitHub
place: the Compare page
show: show where
Opens the same comparison on github.com, each side by the name it has there.
Entry points: **Open on GitHub** in the comparison page (**Compare the two on GitHub, once they are pushed**).
Offered when: the main remote of the repository is on github.com, neither side is the working tree, and the two are not the same commit.
Refuses: when it is not offered: *GitHub does not have the working tree: compare two commits that are pushed.*, *The two sides are the same commit.*, *The main remote of this repository is not on github.com.*
@card desktop compare.open-on-service
title: Open the comparison on another hosting service
place: the Compare page
show: show where
What `compare.open-on-github` does for GitHub, on GitLab, Bitbucket, Azure DevOps, Gitea and Forgejo: opens the same comparison on the hosting service of the main remote, each side by the name it has there.
Entry points: **Open on `<service>`** in the comparison page, `<service>` the name of the service (**Open on GitLab**, **Open on Azure DevOps**), where **Open on GitHub** is for GitHub.
Offered when: the main remote is on one of those services (`hosting.detect-remote`), neither side is the working tree, and the two are not the same commit; on Bitbucket, Gitea and Forgejo, whose pages compare only since the two diverged, only in **Since they diverged**.
Refuses: when it is not offered: *`<service>` does not have the working tree: compare two commits that are pushed.*, *The two sides are the same commit.*, *`<service>` compares only since the two diverged.*, *The main remote of this repository is not on a hosting service Gitardo knows.*

@card desktop file-history.file
title: Show the history of a file
place: the Files page
show: quick search
Narrows the timeline to the commits that changed a file, under every name it had. The graph stays connected, so it still shows on which branches the file changed.
Entry points: **Show file history** in the context menu of a file in the Files page, of the File page and of its title; the history button (a clock with an arrow turning back) next to each file in the inspector; **History of the active file** in the **…** menu of the header; **Show file history** in Quick search, for the File page in front.
Offered when: the file is inside a repository of the workspace. In a workspace with several, the repository that holds the file (the innermost one) is shown first.
Refuses: a file outside every repository of the workspace, *Open a file of this repository to see its history.*
@card desktop file-history.read-committed-lines

Before following lines back, they are found in the commit checked out, since git follows them from a commit, while the file in the File page may have edits not saved or not committed.

@card desktop file-history.map-lines

@card desktop file-history.lines
title: Show the history of some lines
place: the Files page
show: quick search
Narrows the timeline to the commits that changed the lines selected in the File page, or the line of the cursor (`git log -L`). Git follows the lines back from the commit checked out, through moves too.
Entry points: **Show history of selected lines** in the context menu of the File page and in Quick search.
Offered when: the File page in front shows a file on disk inside a repository.
Refuses: - no File page of a file of a repository in front: *Open a file of this repository to see the history of its lines.* - the file is in no commit: *`<file name>` is in no commit yet, so its lines have no history.* - every line selected was added since: *This line was added after the last commit, and has no history yet.* or *These lines were added after the last commit, and have no history yet.*

@card desktop blame.file

place: the Files page

Beside each line of the File page, before its text, the day it was last changed and by whom, with the commit's other lines brought out and the age of each line in color.

@card desktop blame.toggle-file

@card desktop blame.previous
title: Blame the file as it was before a commit
place: the Files page
show: quick search
Opens the file as the commit that last changed a line found it, with the blame it had then: who had written what the commit replaced. Going on from there walks back through the history of a line, past the commits that only moved or reformatted it.
Entry points: **Blame the file as it was before** in the hover of the blame of a line (`blame.file`); **Blame file before commit of current line** in the context menu of the File page and in Quick search.
Offered when: the link, when the commit has a commit before it for this file (`previous` of `blame.read-file`); the command, on a File page of a file of a repository.
Refuses: as `blame.open-line-commit`; and when the commit of the line added the file, *`<short hash>` is the commit that added this file, so there is nothing before it to blame.*
@card desktop blame.ignore-whitespace

show: show where
Removes a worktree's folder with `git worktree remove`, and its branch too if wanted.
Entry points: **Remove…** in the context menu of a worktree; **Remove worktree…** and **Remove worktree and delete branch…** in the menu of a branch another worktree has, which answer the question about the branch.
Offered when: the worktree is not the main worktree, not the one the views show and not the folder of the window.
Refuses: on the folder of the window, **This worktree is the folder open in this window: remove it from another one.**
@card desktop worktree.prune
title: Prune missing worktrees
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Has git forget the worktrees whose folders are gone, which frees their branches.
Entry points: **Prune missing worktrees** in the context menu of a worktree and in the **…** of the Worktrees list.
Offered when: at least one worktree is missing and not locked: git never prunes a locked one (`worktree.locked`).
@card desktop worktree.lock
title: Lock a worktree
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Has git keep a worktree whatever happens to its folder, with the reason why: a worktree on a drive that is not always plugged in is not forgotten by **Prune**, and is not removed or moved by mistake.
Entry points: **Lock…** in the context menu of a worktree in the Worktrees list and of the row of a worktree in **Repositories** (`repo.group-tabs`). The rows of the worktrees in **Repositories** have no context menu yet, so the entry is only in the Worktrees list.
Offered when: the worktree is not the main worktree and is not locked; a missing worktree too, which is what a lock is most for.
Refuses: on the main worktree, as git does: **Git cannot lock the main worktree, the folder the repository was created in.**
@card desktop worktree.unlock
title: Unlock a worktree
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Takes the lock of `worktree.lock` away, so that **Prune**, **Remove…** and **Rename…** treat the worktree as any other.
Entry points: **Unlock** in the context menu of a locked worktree in the Worktrees list and of its row in **Repositories**.
Offered when: the worktree is locked.
@card desktop worktree.locked
title: What a locked worktree shows, and what it changes
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
How a lock is read and shown, and what **Remove…**, **Rename…**, **Prune** and the clean-up of branches do with a locked worktree, which git refuses to remove or move and never prunes.
@card desktop worktree.read-folder-links
title: Read the links to folders outside a worktree
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
On Windows, git deletes what is inside a junction when it removes a worktree: the folder it links to, in the folder the link came from. Elsewhere, a symbolic link that a pattern for folders such as `node_modules/` does not ignore is a file git does not track, and `git worktree remove` refuses the worktree. This read finds those links, so that they are taken out first.
@card desktop worktree.recover-held-folder
title: Get past a folder that Windows holds
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
On Windows another program can hold a worktree's folder for a moment, such as a window or a terminal that has just left it. A move or a removal that fails for that is not left half done.
@card desktop worktree.remove-empty-folders
title: Leave no empty folders behind
place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
After a worktree is moved or removed, the folders that held only it go too, up to the folder of the worktrees.
@card desktop worktree.branch-in-another-worktree

place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: none
Git checks a branch out in one worktree at a time, so a branch that another worktree has is worked on there. This is how the branch rail, the timeline and the menus treat it.
@card desktop worktree.open-in-worktree

place: Kept aside, in the branch rail
show: show where
Works on a branch in its own worktree, with nothing asked: the worktree is made if it is not there yet, and opens.
Entry points: **Open in worktree** in the context menu of a local branch and of a remote branch; a double click on a branch while `branches.openIn` is `worktree`, where **Open in worktree** also comes before **Check out** in its menu.
Offered when: the branch is not checked out here and no other worktree has it (for a remote branch: its local branch of the same name).
Refuses: **`<branch>` is checked out in this window.**; **The worktree of `<branch>` is missing from `<folder>`: prune the worktrees to free the branch.**; **`<folder>` already exists.**

@card desktop patch.copy

place: the timeline

Copies one commit, or several selected, as one patch, for `git am` to make them again in another repository or on another branch.
Entry points: **Copy as patch** in the context menu of a commit in the timeline; in the bar above the commits of the timeline and in the context menu of several selected commits (`selection.bar`).

@card desktop patch.check

place: the timeline
show: none
What applying a patch with `--3way` would do, without doing it: the files it would leave in conflict, or what keeps it from applying at all.
@card desktop patch.apply
title: Apply a patch
place: the timeline
show: quick search
Applies a patch from a file or from the clipboard: as commits, each made again on top of the current branch with its author and message, or to the working tree only, its changes staged and nothing committed.
Entry points: Quick search: **Apply Patch…**; **Apply patch…** in the **…** menu of the header.
Refuses: - a text with no line starting with `diff --git ` or `--- ` followed by a name: **`<file name>` has no patch in it.**, or **There is no patch in the clipboard.**; - as commits while a merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert or am is under way: **Finish or abort the `<operation>` first.**; - as commits with something staged, which `git am` would refuse half way and leave to be aborted: **git am applies patches only while nothing is staged: commit or unstage the staged changes first.**; - a patch that does not apply, from `patch.check`: a warning **`<Name>` does not apply, so nothing was changed.**, with the first 15 reasons of git and **…and K more**. Nothing is changed.
@card desktop patch.am-stopped

place: the timeline
show: none
When `git am` stops because a commit of the patch does not fit, it waits to be continued, skipped or aborted, as a cherry-pick does.
Entry points: the banner of the operation in the timeline and the Changes page, shown while `git am` is under way: the repository is in an am when `.git/rebase-apply/applying` exists, which is checked before the markers of a rebase.

place: the branch rail
show: none
The open merge requests of the project on GitLab, each with the state of the pipeline of its last commit, by the name of its branch: what `github.read-pull-requests` gives for GitHub, for the same labels.
@card desktop hosting.read-pull-request-list
title: Read the open pull requests to pick from
place: the branch rail
show: none
The open pull requests of the repository, those from forks included, with their number, title, author and branch, for the pick of `hosting.checkout-pull-request`.
@card desktop hosting.checkout-pull-request
title: Check out a pull request
place: the branch rail
show: quick search
Checks out the commits of a pull request in a local branch, also one from a fork, to try it or review it.
Entry points: **Check out a pull request…** (**Check out a merge request…** on GitLab) in Quick search and in the **…** menu of the header; **Check out pull request #`<n>`** in the context menu of a remote branch whose label shows a pull request (`graph.pull-request-badge`), in the timeline and in the branch rail.
Offered when: the main remote is on GitHub, GitLab, Gitea or Forgejo, which keep the commits of each pull request under a reference of their own; no merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert, patch or bisect is under way.
Refuses: *Finish or abort the `<operation>` first.*; *End the bisect first.*; "`<name>` is checked out in another worktree: check out the pull request there." when it is. What is typed in the pick and is neither a pull request listed nor a number is refused while it is typed: "Type the number of a pull request".
@card desktop preview.confirm
title: Show the commands before they run
place: the header and the bottom panel
show: none
Before a plan runs, the engine decides from `commandPreview` and the plan's danger whether to show it, and waits for the user's answer.
Entry points: every operation, through the engine, which decides with.
@card desktop log.record
title: Log every command that changes something
place: the header and the bottom panel
show: none
Every step of every plan is written to the command log before it starts, and its result after.
@card desktop log.show
title: Show the command log
place: the header and the bottom panel
show: none
The **Command log**, a tab of the bottom panel, lists what the log recorded, newest last, and follows it as it grows.
Entry points: **Command log** in the bottom panel (`panel.bottom`), the tab beside **Activity**; **Show Command Log** in Quick search.
@card desktop undo.last
title: Undo the last operation
place: the header and the bottom panel
show: quick search
**Undo** takes back the newest operation of Gitardo, as its journal keeps it: a commit, amend, checkout, merge, pull, rebase or reset by the reflog, and what the reflog does not see from the journal itself. With nothing in the journal, after a commit made in a terminal for one, it takes back the last move of the reflog. When the newest operation is one it cannot take back, a push, a fetch, a tag, a stash or a worktree, it says so rather than take back the one before it. It always shows what it is about to do first. With nothing in the journal, undoing twice redoes.
Entry points: **Undo** in the **…** menu of the header; **Undo Last Operation** in Quick search; the **Undo** of the messages that follow an operation.
Offered when: the journal has an entry, or the reflog of `HEAD` has a previous position.
@card desktop undo.journal
title: Remember what Undo needs
place: the header and the bottom panel
show: none
The engine keeps, for each repository, a journal of Gitardo's operations, newest last, each with what Undo needs of it: a move of `HEAD` the reflog takes back, the commit a branch was at, a branch created, what was checked out before a branch with no history (`branch.create-orphan`, whose move git writes in no reflog), the commit of a stash, the files whose conflicts were resolved, the `HEAD` before a series, or why it cannot be taken back. It holds while `HEAD` is what the newest entry left, and is emptied once `HEAD` moves by something Gitardo did not do. An operation that cannot be taken back and changed only files and the index (staging, a save, a discard) adds nothing, and Undo goes past it. It is kept in memory: a new engine starts with an empty journal, and Undo then goes by the reflog.
@card desktop undo.moved-branches
title: Take back the branches a rewrite moved
place: the header and the bottom panel
show: none
The journal entry of a rewrite that moved the other branches of a stack keeps where each was, so that one Undo puts them back with the branch rewritten.
@card desktop activity.track
title: Say what is running
place: the header and the bottom panel
show: none
Every read, plan, comparison and request that may take longer than a frame is registered while it runs, with what it is in words, when it started and what asked for it, so that the activity indicator of the status bar (`activity.status-bar`) and the **Activity** tab (`activity.what-is-running`) can say it. The words are those of `activity.describe-task`.
@card desktop diff.page
title: The Diff page
place: the Diff and Merge pages
show: page
Two versions of a file side by side, kept level, with every change joined across the gutter to where it lands.
Entry points: a click on a file of the changes unless `changes.openOnClick` is `file`, and **Open changes** of the menu of its row; a file in the inspector, beside it (`diff.beside-inspector`); a file in the Commit page and in a comparison; **Compare with the saved file** in the Changes page; **Compare with…** on a file; the button on the header of the Changes page that opens all its diffs in one page; **Show the result** in the review of the conflicts; `conflicts.open-file` for a file deleted on one side; a deleted file of the Files page (`file-view.files-changes`).
Refuses: a file with a NUL byte on either side: *This file looks binary.* A diff that takes more than 5 seconds to compute: *This file has too many differences to display.*
@card desktop diff.beside-inspector

A file of the commit in the inspector opens its diff where the timeline and the side column were, and the inspector stays on the right as the column of the commit's files, so that every file of the commit can be read one after the other without it closing.
Entry points: a click on a file in the inspector, of a commit, a stash, the draft or a comparison in its place (`compare.in-place`); `Alt+Down` and `Alt+Up` in the Diff page opened so, and `Down` and `Up` in the tree of the inspector beside it, for the next and the previous file of the inspector.

@card desktop diff.staged-changes
title: Which changes are in the index already
place: the Diff and Merge pages
show: none
Tells, in a diff of the working tree against `HEAD`, which changes are staged, so that they are drawn dimmed and offer to be unstaged.
@card desktop diff.change-patch
title: The patch of one change
place: the Diff and Merge pages
show: none
The patch that moves one change into the index, or back out of it, given to `git apply --cached` on standard input.
@card desktop diff.stage-change
title: Stage this change
place: the Diff and Merge pages
show: show where
Puts one change of the working tree into the index.
Entry points: the **+** button (**Stage this change**) on the band of a change in the gutter.
Offered when: the right side is the working tree and the left is `HEAD` or the index, the file is not renamed, and the change is not staged.
Refuses: when the file changed since the page was computed: *The file changed since the diff was shown. Try again.* When the file is not UTF-8 and the patch has a character above 127: *Staging a change needs UTF-8 text: stage the whole file.* When the patch starts at line 0 or 1 and the file starts with a byte order mark: *The first line of a file with a byte order mark cannot be staged as a change: stage the whole file.* When no difference between the index and the working tree touches the change: *This change is staged already.*
@card desktop diff.unstage-change
title: Unstage this change
place: the Diff and Merge pages
show: show where
Takes one change back out of the index.
Entry points: the **−** button (**Unstage this change**) on a staged change of the working tree against `HEAD` (its tooltip **Staged — unstage this change**), and on every change of the index against `HEAD`.
Offered when: the file is not renamed and the change is staged.
Refuses: as `diff.stage-change`; when no difference between the index and `HEAD` touches the change: *Nothing of this change is staged.*
@card desktop diff.revert-change
title: Revert this change
place: the Diff and Merge pages
show: show where

Entry points: the **⟲** button (**Revert this change**) on the band of a change in the gutter.
Offered when: the right side is the working tree (against `HEAD`, the index or a commit).
Refuses: *The file changed since the diff was shown. Try again.*

@card desktop diff.stage-lines

Puts into the index only the lines selected of the changes of the working tree.

Offered when: the right side is the working tree and the left is `HEAD` or the index, the file is not renamed, and some line selected differs between the index and the working tree.

Try again.*; for a file that is not UTF-8 with a character above 127 in the patch, *Staging lines needs UTF-8 text: stage the whole file.*; for the first line of a file with a byte order mark, *The first line of a file with a byte order mark cannot be staged alone: stage the whole file.* When no line selected differs between the index and the working tree: *These lines are staged already.*

@card desktop lfs.read-status

@card desktop lfs.read-files

The files of the commit checked out that the repository stores with Git LFS, with their sizes and whether their content is downloaded.
@card desktop lfs.pointer
title: A file left as a pointer
place: the Files page
show: none
Tells whether some bytes are a pointer of Git LFS, and which content they stand for.
@card desktop lfs.read-content
title: The content of a file stored with Git LFS, from the store of the repository
place: the Files page
show: none
The content a pointer stands for, read from the store of Git LFS on this computer, never downloaded by a read.
@card desktop lfs.show-in-pages
title: A file stored with Git LFS, in the pages
place: the Files page
show: none
What the File page, the Diff page and the page of a commit show of a file stored with Git LFS: its content when it is on this computer, and otherwise that it is not downloaded, with **Download**, never the text of its pointer.
Entry points: drawn by the File page, the Diff page and the page of a commit in place of the text of a file whose version is a pointer (`lfs.pointer`) at a path the repository stores with Git LFS.
@card desktop lfs.download
title: Download files stored with Git LFS
place: the Files page
show: show where
Downloads the content of files left as pointers, into the working tree for the commit checked out, or into the store for another commit.
Entry points: **Download** where `lfs.show-in-pages` says a file is not downloaded; **Download** in the menu of a file of the **Large files** page; **Download all** on that page; **Download** in the context menu of a file or a folder of the Files page that holds files left as pointers.
Offered when: Git LFS is installed (`lfs.read-status`).
Refuses: without Git LFS: **Git LFS is not installed: install it from git-lfs.com, then try again.** with **Open git-lfs.com**; a path holding `,` or a character of patterns, which `--include` cannot name alone: **`<path>` cannot be named to Git LFS on its own: download all the files instead.**.
@card desktop lfs.page
title: The Large files page
place: the Files page
show: page
A page with what the repository stores with Git LFS: whether Git LFS is there and set up, the patterns, the files with their sizes and whether they are downloaded, and the locks.
Entry points: **Large files…** in the context menu of the row of a repository in **Repositories** (`repo.tabs`); **Large Files (Git LFS)…** in Quick search.
@card desktop lfs.set-up
title: Set up Git LFS for a repository
place: the Files page
show: show where
Sets up the filter and the hooks of Git LFS for one repository, so that its files are downloaded at checkout and stored with Git LFS at commit.
Entry points: **Set up** on the **Large files** page.
Offered when: Git LFS is installed, the repository has patterns, and it is not set up (`lfs.read-status`).
@card desktop lfs.track
title: Store files with Git LFS
place: the Files page
show: show where
Has Git LFS store the files that a pattern takes, from their next commit on.
Entry points: **Store with Git LFS…** in the context menu of a file of the Files page and in the menu of a file of the changes (`changes.row-menu`); **Track a pattern…** on the **Large files** page.
Offered when: Git LFS is installed and set up for the repository, and no pattern takes the file already.
Refuses: without Git LFS, as `lfs.download`; not set up: **Git LFS is not set up in this repository.** with **Set up**.
@card desktop lfs.store-committed
title: Store with Git LFS the files committed before
place: the Files page
show: show where
Stages again, as pointers, the files git tracks that a pattern of Git LFS takes but that were committed before the pattern was there, so that the next commit stores them with Git LFS. The commits before keep them as they are.
Entry points: **Store them with Git LFS in the next commit** in the message after `lfs.track`, and in the menu of a pattern of the **Large files** page.
Offered when: such files exist.
@card desktop lfs.untrack
title: Stop storing a pattern with Git LFS
place: the Files page
show: show where
Takes a pattern out of the `.gitattributes` at the top, so that the files it took are stored by git from their next commit on.
Entry points: **Stop storing with Git LFS** in the menu of a pattern of the **Large files** page.
Offered when: Git LFS is installed and the pattern is in the `.gitattributes` at the top; a pattern of another file has **Open `<file>`** only.

Refuses: git-lfs's message when the server refuses, such as a file locked by someone else meanwhile.

Offered when: the file is locked.

@card extension repo.read-state

place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none

In VS Code: `read/repo.read-state` of Gitardo's engine, from `Reads.parts` in, with `refresh` (version 1.8 of the protocol) for **Refresh** and for a repository chosen: the engine keeps what each read counted in the other worktrees, and counts again only those whose git folder changed. `repository/open` counts them all. `gitardo.readOnStartup` is the setting `readOnStartup`.

@card extension repo.watch-changes
title: Which changes ask for a read
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
The repository is read again on its own when it or its files change, from Gitardo, a terminal or another program. This says which changes count, and how soon.
In VS Code: the watchers of in, of the common git folder and of the working tree. A file of the working tree in the folder of another worktree of `RepoState.worktrees` inside the repository asks for nothing; the others of a burst are given, a second after the first of them, to `read/repo.watch-changes` of Gitardo's engine (version 1.8), which leaves out those in nested worktrees and those `git check-ignore` ignores, a `.gitignore` never, and the repository is read at once when one is left. A file saved, created, deleted or renamed in VS Code's editor stands for Gitardo's own editor. VS Code getting the focus stands for the window getting it, and a change that came while no view of Gitardo was open for one that came while it was minimized; the minute is counted from the end of the last read of the repository shown.
@card extension repo.schedule-reads
title: Read again when something changed
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
Decides when the repository is read: once after a burst of changes, never twice at once, again while something keeps changing during each read, never during an operation. **Refresh** asks for a read at once.
In VS Code: and in, with the status bar item of `activity.status-bar`; **Refresh** of the views and **Gitardo: Refresh** are, which reads every commit and counts every worktree again. `EngineGraph` of keeps the commits of a read while the commit checked out, the references and the stashes are those of the read before.

@card extension repo.tabs
title: The repositories open, in the side column
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
**Repositories**, the first section of the side column: a row for each repository open, the rows of its worktrees and its submodules under it, the one shown marked, what each is on, and **+** to open another.
In VS Code: **Gitardo Repositories**, a view of the Source Control side bar above Gitardo's other views, or **Gitardo: Show Repositories**, named as the views that have a namesake of VS Code's beside them are: the same rows for the repositories Gitardo found in the workspace (`repo.read-repositories`), without **+**, **×** and the keys of the tabs, which are VS Code's workspace to decide (to). A row is 22 pixels high, as a row of a list of VS Code; the one Gitardo shows has the background of a row selected and a bar on its left, a click on a row shows it (`repo.select`), and nothing is chosen while an operation runs, the row of its repository showing a spinner. When the name and what it is on do not both fit, each gives way in proportion, the name cut at its start and the branch at its end, the counts kept whole. `Tab` moves to the rows, the arrows from one to the next and fold or unfold, `Enter` or `Space` is a click; the rows are a tree for the screen reader, each said with what it is on, the one shown as selected. The folders are followed by the engine only once the view is opened, and are told of changes only while it is in sight: what changed while it was folded is said when it is seen again. The context menu of a row has **Open in terminal**, **Reveal in File Explorer** as the system calls it, **Show in Gitardo** when it is not the one shown, and **Copy path**; the row of a worktree of the repository Gitardo shows has the menu that worktree has in the Branches view, and the row of a submodule of it the menu of that submodule, both made of the state of the repository shown, which is why the rows of the other repositories have the four alone. The view and VS Code's own Repositories view follow each other as `repo.follow-source-control` says, a click on a row being a `repo.select`. `RepositoriesApp` in, `repositoryRowMenu` in, `RepositoriesView` in, the rows in; tested by, and the step "lists the repositories in the Repositories view with their worktrees and what each is on, counts a file changed in one not shown, and shows the row clicked" of.

@card extension repo.worktree-summary
title: What a repository or a worktree is on
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
The branch checked out in a repository or a worktree, how many files are changed there, and how many commits are to pull and to push, for the rows of **Repositories** and the rows of the worktrees and the submodules under them.

The folder Gitardo shows is not said to the engine: its row takes its summary from the state of the views (`summaryOfState`), and it is read by the engine once it is left. Tested by, and.

@card extension repo.keep-open
title: Keep what each repository showed
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
What a repository keeps while another is shown, so that coming back to it finds it as it was, at once.
In VS Code: with `gitardo.repositories.keepOpen` on, the repository left by the list of `repo.select`, by VS Code's Repositories view (`repo.follow-source-control`) or by **Show in Gitardo** keeps, in memory, the scroll of the graph, the commit selected and its details, the search, the branch filter, the references with their sections open or closed, and the message of the Changes view; the engine keeps its state as last read. Coming back shows that at once, and reads again behind it when it changed meanwhile. Tested by `test/e2e/ui.mjs`, step "with keeping repositories open on, a repository chosen again in the list shows the commit it had selected, at the same scroll";, the steps that start "with keeping repositories open" and "turning keeping repositories open off", and "lists the worktrees of a repository of the workspace under it in the list of the repositories"; and.
@card extension repo.group-tabs

@card extension repo.watch-hidden
title: Keep the tabs not shown up to date
place: Repositories, in the side column
show: none
How the repositories of the tabs not shown follow their changes without reading at every one of them.
In VS Code: with `gitardo.repositories.keepOpen` on, the repositories the extension keeps follow these rules, the window of VS Code taking the place of the tab shown. The repositories it keeps are those left since the window opened: none is read in the background, so the rule of the reads one after the other once the workspace opens has nothing to read there. Their watchers stay, and a change marks them out of date. Tested by, steps "with keeping repositories open on, a commit made in a terminal in a repository not shown reads nothing, and showing it reads it once" and "with keeping repositories open on, shows a repository chosen again at once as it was left, and reads nothing for it when nothing changed". What the rows of its Repositories view say of the repositories not shown is not their state: it is read by the engine in the background, as `repo.worktree-summary` says, whatever the setting, and never while an operation runs or a state is read.

@card extension graph.read-commits
title: Read the commits of the graph
place: the timeline
show: none
The commits the timeline lists, newest first, with their parents, a page at a time as git prints them: one read goes on to the end of the history, or to 100,000 commits.
In VS Code: `read/graph.read-commits` of Gitardo's engine with `stream` (version 1.8 of the protocol), each page in a notification `read/graph.rows` as it is read; `EngineGraph` of keeps one read for each repository, adds its pages as they come, and stops it when another history is asked for. The same history read again, after a commit, a fetch, a branch or a tag made, on **Refresh** (`readAgain`) or after a change of the settings, goes over the commits listed: while its pages are the same commits, they stay, laid out as they are, and the views are sent none of them; from the first that is not, the commits are cut there and those read follow, laid out again; a read that ends with fewer leaves as many (milestone M25). Tested by, "keeps every row, and gives the views none, when a read after a branch was made finds the same commits", "reads every commit again on Refresh, and keeps the rows of those that are the same", "cuts the rows where a read finds other commits, as after a commit made on top, and lays them out as a first read would" and "takes out the commits the history no longer has when a read of it ends with fewer".
@card extension graph.read-position
title: Where a commit is in the history listed
place: the timeline
show: none
How many commits come before a commit in the history the timeline lists, to know how far it must be loaded for the commit to be there; or that it is not in it.
@card extension graph.view
title: The timeline
place: the timeline
show: none

@card extension graph.islands
title: The islands of the window
place: the timeline
show: none
The side column, the timeline, the inspector and the page shown are each a rounded card on the ground of the window, under the header.
In VS Code: with `gitardo.graph.islands` on, the references, the commits and the details of the Graph view are each an island, on a ground one step darker than the panel's background in the dark themes and one step deeper in the light ones, with the radius and the border of VS Code's widgets and a gap of 8 pixels between them, 4 in a panel narrower than 900 pixels; the gap is the line dragged to resize them, and the details under the commits in a narrow panel are an island too. The toolbar stays above them, on the ground. Off, the view is as it was. The other views keep their look. The class `islands` of the Graph view's `App` and the rules under it in; the ground is mixed from `--vscode-panel-background` with black. Tested by `test/e2e/ui.mjs`, step "with islands on, the references, the commits and the details are rounded islands on a darker ground, the gap between two is dragged to resize them, and off the graph is as it was".

@card extension graph.load-more
title: Load the next commits while scrolling
place: the timeline
show: none
The commits come a page at a time as they are read, without waiting for the list to be scrolled, so that scrolling does not stop to wait for them; past the 100,000 commits of one read, the next read starts long before the end of those shown.
In VS Code: the pages `EngineGraph` adds are laid out and sent by to the views that list commits, the graph and Gitardo Graph, those that come within 150 ms together, with only the rows the views do not have; the other views, which the rows of a page change nothing of, are sent nothing; `loadMore` of the list of commits asks for nothing while a read adds its pages, and past 100,000 commits reads from the last one. The pages that come while the rest of the state is read, the newest entry of the journal among it, are laid out under the rows right before the views are given the state (`completed` of), so that a read that ends meanwhile, as in a repository of a little more than a page, leaves no commit waiting for the list to be scrolled (milestone M25). Tested by, "has every commit, and no more to come, when the read ends while the rest of the state is read". While the commits of the history listed are read again from their first page, after a commit, a fetch or **Refresh**, the list keeps the length it had until the read reaches it or ends, so that it stays at the row it is scrolled to: `held` of says how long, and under the commits read so far the rows the list had before are drawn where they were, dimmed after 150 ms, without their labels and taking no click, until the read reaches them. Another repository, other branches, another filter or other parents followed start from the first page as before (`listingOf`), and a read that ends with fewer commits leaves the list as long as they are. `loadMore` asks for nothing either once the read ended at the end of the history, which a list asked for while the last page came. Tested by, "keeps its length and the width of its graph when Refresh reads its commits again from the first page, until they are back"; in a window by `test/perf/graph-load.mjs`, which reports the row the list is at.
@card extension graph.lanes
title: Lay out the lines of the graph
place: the timeline
show: none
Where each commit's dot goes, and the lines between commits, row by row.
In VS Code: `GraphLayout` of, whose `page` lays out a page under the rows before it from the lanes under the last of them; `LaidOutRows` of keeps the layout with the rows of a read. Folded merges are laid out again whole in the view (`foldMerges`).
@card extension graph.fit-width
title: How wide the graph is drawn
place: the timeline
show: none
The graph is as wide as its lines need, within limits, and draws its lines closer before it cuts any.
In VS Code: `fitGraph` of. While the commits of the history listed are read again from their first page, the most lanes are those the list had before the read, or those of the rows read when they are more, so that the graph does not get narrower with the first page and wider again (; `held` of); the first read of a history widens the graph as its pages come. Tested by, "keeps its length and the width of its graph when Refresh reads its commits again from the first page, until they are back".

@card extension filter.show-too
title: Show this history too
place: the header, Search and Quick search
show: none
Adds a branch, remote branch or tag to the ones shown, when the timeline is narrowed already.
Entry points: **Show this history too** in the context menu of a branch, remote branch or tag (on its label in the timeline, in the branch rail); the eye on a dimmed reference of the branch rail (`filter.list-too`).
Offered when: the timeline is narrowed and the reference is not among those shown.
@card extension filter.hide
title: Hide this history
place: the header, Search and Quick search
show: none
Takes a branch, remote branch or tag out of the ones shown.
Entry points: **Hide this history** in the context menu of a branch, remote branch or tag (on its label in the timeline, in the branch rail).
Offered when: the timeline is narrowed, the reference is among those shown and is not the only one.
@card extension filter.show-all
title: Show all branches
place: the header, Search and Quick search
show: none
Shows everything again.
Entry points: **Show all branches** in the context menu of a branch, remote branch or tag while the timeline is narrowed; **Show all branches** on the bar above the commits (`filter.bar`); **Show all** on the line at the top of the branch rail (`refs.scope-line`); a second click on the branch the timeline is narrowed to in the branch rail (`refs.tree`); **All** in the branch filter.
Offered when: the timeline is narrowed to some branches.
@card extension filter.bar
title: The bar of what the timeline is narrowed to
place: the header, Search and Quick search
show: none
While the timeline is narrowed, a bar above the commits says to what, with the way to undo it.
Entry points: above the commits of the timeline, while the timeline is narrowed to some branches, to a search shown as a list, to the history of a file, a folder or some lines, or to the history of a commit on no branch.
@card extension filter.log-arguments
title: Which history the timeline lists
place: the header, Search and Quick search
show: none
The arguments of `git log` that say which commits the timeline lists, from the branches shown, in which order: everything but the format, the count, and what a search or the history of a file adds.
@card extension filter.remember
title: The branches shown, remembered per repository
place: the header, Search and Quick search
show: none
The choice is one for the timeline, wide or narrow, and is remembered for each repository of the workspace. A branch that is deleted is forgotten.

@card extension details.read-commit
title: Read the details of a commit
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
Everything the inspector, the Stashes list and the page of a commit show of one commit: its message, author, committer, dates, parents and the files it changed, and, for a stash, the untracked files it took.
@card extension details.read-signature
title: Read the signature of a commit
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
Whether a commit is signed, and what git says of its signature: good, bad, made by a key it does not trust or cannot check, and by whom.
In VS Code: `read/details.read-signature` of the engine (protocol 1.13), asked by the details of the graph and the tab of a commit (`Reads.signature` in).
@card extension details.show
title: Details of the selected commit
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
The summary of the commit selected in the timeline, in the inspector beside the commits or over them when the window is narrow: its message, who made it and when, its hash and parents, and the files it changed, each one click away from its diff.
Entry points: selecting a commit, a stash or the draft in the timeline; the same in the narrow layout of the window, where the inspector opens over the commits.
@card extension details.signature
title: The signature of a commit
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
Says, in the inspector and in the tab of a commit, whether the commit is signed and whether the signature holds.
In VS Code: the same line in the details beside the graph and in the tab of a commit, drawn by `SignatureLine` of with the words of, its icon the Codicon `shield`. Each view asks with `getSignature` once the details are drawn, and the extension cancels the read it asked before for that view.
@card extension details.message-title
title: The title of a message
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
A commit message whose first paragraph is long, often a message written as one paragraph with no title, would fill the inspector with bold text: its title becomes its first sentence, or its first words, and the rest goes on under it.
@card extension details.message-unwrap
title: Lines of a message put back together
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
Messages are written to fit in 72 columns; shown in a narrower or wider place they would break twice a line. The lines that were cut to fit are joined again, so that the text wraps at the width of the inspector or of the page.
@card extension details.show-more
title: Show more of a long message
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
A long message in the inspector shows its first lines only, so that the files stay in sight under it; **Show more** shows it whole, in place.
Entry points: the link under the text of the message in the inspector.
Offered when: the text under the title is longer than four lines at the width of the inspector.
@card extension details.file-tree
title: Files as a tree of directories
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
The files of a commit, of a comparison or of the changes, laid out under their directories, which fold.
@card extension details.show-all-files
title: The first 300 files, and Show all
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
A commit can change thousands of files, and a row for each makes the list slow: a list of more than 300 files shows the first 300 and a link for the rest.
Entry points: the end of the files in the inspector, in the page of a commit, in the comparison page and in the in-place comparison, and of the Changes page.
@card extension details.compare-file-with
title: Compare a file with a branch, tag or commit
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: show where
Compares one file, as a commit, a comparison or the working tree has it, with the branch, tag, commit or working tree the user chooses, in the Diff page.
Entry points: **Compare with…** in the context menu of a file in the inspector, in the page of a commit (in the list and on the header of its diff), in the comparison page and in the in-place comparison.
@card extension details.stash
title: Details of a stash
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
A stash selected shows what it holds, as a commit does, including the untracked files it took, which git keeps apart from the rest.
Entry points: selecting a stash in the timeline or in the Stashes list of the branch rail; opening its page.
@card extension commits.copy-hash
title: Copy the hash of a commit
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: show where
Puts the full hash of a commit on the clipboard.
Entry points: **Copy hash** in the context menu of a commit in the timeline, narrowed or not; a click on the hash in the inspector and in the page of a commit.
@card extension commit-page.open
title: Open a commit as a page
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: quick search
Opens a commit whole, in a page of its own, or shows the page it has.
Entry points: double-click on a commit in the timeline, narrowed or not, or in its narrow layout, or `Enter` on the commit selected; the button beside the one that closes the inspector; **Open as a page** in the context menu of a commit; a parent in the page of a commit, a side, a commit or the merge base in the comparison page; Quick search: **Open Commit as a Page…**.
Offered when: on a commit or a stash; not on the draft. A double click on the label of a branch checks the branch out instead (`branch.checkout`).
Refuses: a hash that is not a commit of the repository: **`<short hash>` is not a commit of this repository.**
@card extension commit-page.read-patch
title: Read the lines a commit changed
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
What a commit changed in each of its files, as lines, for its page; the same lines serve the comparison page.
@card extension commit-page.diff-lines
title: The lines of each diff, and how many are sent
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
Turns the output of a diff into files with their hunks, the lines each gained and lost, and the cap of 20,000 lines a page is given.
@card extension commit-page.view
title: The page of a commit
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
A commit whole, on a page: its full message, labels, author, committer, dates, hash and parents, the files it changed with the lines each gained and lost, and, on demand, what changed in each, in the colors of the code.
Entry points: `commit-page.open`.
@card extension commit-page.initial-folds
title: Which diffs start folded
place: the inspector and the Commit page
show: none
A page of many or long diffs would take long to show and be hard to read: the long ones start folded.

The two versions of an image that a commit changed, drawn in its page and in a comparison in place of **Binary file.**.
In VS Code: the page of a commit and the tab of a comparison, Gitardo's webview, with the two versions as images of `data:` URIs from the bytes of `read/diff.read-sides`.

@card extension changes.pane
title: The Changes page
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: page
The page where the changes are staged and committed: one list of the changed files, each with a box, the files in conflict apart above it, and the commit box at the top, whose message and **Commit** the draft at the top of the timeline shows too. The rest of the commit box, **Amend last commit**, the button that drafts the message with the AI provider and the identity, is on this page only. While the timeline is narrowed to branches that do not have the commit checked out, the draft is not there, and this page is where one commits.
In VS Code: the **Gitardo Changes** view of the Source Control side bar (`ChangesApp` in, view kind `changes`, registered as `gitardo.changes`), declared hidden in `package.json`, so that VS Code shows it only once **Gitardo: Show Changes** in the Command Palette (`gitardo.openChanges`), ticking **Gitardo Changes** in the **…** menu of the side bar, or **Show Changes** in the message after a squash merge asks for it. Its rows, boxes and buttons are those of this page, with VS Code's editors in place of the pages (`changes.open`); a list of more than 300 files shows the first 300 and **Show all N files…** (`FILES_LIMIT` in). The view runs nothing itself: what its boxes and buttons ask is sent to Gitardo's engine as the cards of the operations say. **View as tree** or **View as list**, and the message of the commit box, are kept in the view's state (`loadUiState` and `saveUiState` in), the layout as `changesFiles`. The placeholder of the message is, or `on detached HEAD`; a click on the identity under it runs, still in TypeScript. Before the repository is read the view says *Loading repository…*.
@card extension changes.read-working-tree
title: Read what is not committed
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
What git says of each changed file: staged, not staged, untracked or in conflict.
@card extension changes.list
title: One list of the changed files
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
Turns what git says into the one list of the Changes page: each file once, as it is next to the last commit, with how much of it is staged.
@card extension changes.unsaved
title: Files with edits not saved
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
Which files of the repository have edits typed in one of Gitardo's own editors and not saved yet: the File page and the editable sides of the Diff and Merge pages.
In VS Code: the files of VS Code's text documents with edits not saved, worked out by the extension itself (and in) by the same rules, with the files git ignores asked of the engine (`reads.ignored` in), and told to the Changes view with an `unsaved` message. It is worked out again at each edit, at each editor closed or saved and at each read of the repository, and sent only when the list changed.
@card extension changes.open
title: What a file of the Changes page opens
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
Decides which page, and which two versions, a click on a file of the Changes page, or **Open changes** and **Open file** of its menu (`changes.row-menu`), opens.
In VS Code: a click on a file of the Changes view, its buttons **Open File** and **Open Changes**, **Open all changes in one editor** on the header of **Changes**, and **Compare with the saved file** on a file only an editor has changed, done by the extension itself (the messages `openChange`, `openFile`, `openDiff`, `openChanges` and `compareWithSaved` in, and `sides` in), with VS Code's editors in place of the pages. The diff opens in VS Code's diff editor, titled or; when VS Code's Git extension has the repository open, its two sides are that extension's own `git:` documents, so that **Stage Selected Ranges**, **Unstage Selected Ranges** and **Revert Selected Ranges** work in it. **Open all changes in one editor** runs `vscode.changes`: one editor titled **Changes**, where each file has the two sides **Open Changes** gives it, rather than the last commit against the file on disk. **Compare with the saved file** runs VS Code's `workbench.files.action.compareWithSaved`. A file in conflict with its base and both sides opens in VS Code's merge editor. The setting is `gitardo.changes.openOnClick`.

@card extension changes.row-menu
title: The menu of a file of the changes
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
What is done to one file of the changes, in the context menu of its row: the rows have no buttons, so that a name is read whole in a narrow column, and keep their box.
In VS Code: the rows of the **Gitardo Changes** view keep these actions as buttons beside the name (`changes.pane`), and have the same menu with a right click (`changedFileMenu` in): the entries of a changed file in this order, those of a file only an editor has changed, and those of a file in conflict, which are its buttons; the entries that change something cannot be chosen while an operation runs. The buttons stay.
@card extension changes.stage
title: Stage a file
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Puts the changes of a file, or of several files given at once, in the index, for the next commit.
In VS Code: ticking the box of a file in the Changes view (titled *Click to stage*, or *Partly staged: click to stage all of it*); **Mark as resolved** on a file in conflict there; ticking the header box while files are in conflict, which gives the files not all staged. Each sends `feature/run` of `changes.stage` to Gitardo's engine (in), on the one file or on the files given (`engineRuns` in); the plan is shown in a modal dialog when `gitardo.commandPreview` is `all` (`VscodeAsker` in). **Stage File** in the title of the Flow Diff sends `changes.stage` on its file, through. When the engine's binary is missing, it says so.
@card extension changes.unstage
title: Unstage a file
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Takes the changes of a file, or of several files given at once, out of the index; the files on disk are not touched.
In VS Code: unticking the ticked box of a file in the Changes view (titled *Staged: click to unstage*), which gives both names of a staged rename; unticking the header box while files are in conflict, which gives the files staged. Each sends `feature/run` of `changes.unstage` to Gitardo's engine, on the one file or on the files given (`engineRuns` in), its plan shown in a modal dialog when `gitardo.commandPreview` is `all` (`VscodeAsker`). **Unstage File** in the title of the Flow Diff sends `changes.unstage` on its file, through. When the engine's binary is missing, it says so.
@card extension changes.stage-all
title: Stage all changes
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Stages every change at once.
In VS Code: ticking the header box of **Changes** in the Changes view (titled *Click to stage all changes*) while no file is in conflict, and **Stage all changes** in the context menu of the uncommitted changes row of the graph, send `feature/run` of `changes.stage-all` to Gitardo's engine on the changes (`engineRuns` in); while files are in conflict the header box sends `changes.stage` with the files not all staged. The plan is shown in a modal dialog when `gitardo.commandPreview` is `all` (`VscodeAsker`). When the engine's binary is missing, it says so.
@card extension changes.unstage-all
title: Unstage all changes
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Takes everything out of the index; the files on disk are not touched.
In VS Code: unticking the ticked header box of **Changes** in the Changes view (titled *All staged: click to unstage all changes*) while no file is in conflict, and **Unstage all changes** in the context menu of the uncommitted changes row of the graph, offered while something is staged, also while files are in conflict, send `feature/run` of `changes.unstage-all` to Gitardo's engine on the changes (`engineRuns` in): neither ends a merge or a cherry-pick any more. While files are in conflict the header box sends `changes.unstage` with the files staged, both names of a rename. The plan is shown in a modal dialog when `gitardo.commandPreview` is `all` (`VscodeAsker`). When the engine's binary is missing, it says so.

@card extension changes.pathspec
title: A file named as itself
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
How a file is named to the git commands that take patterns, so that a name that is also a pattern means only that file.
@card extension commit.create
title: Commit
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Makes a commit of what is staged, with the message of the commit box.
In VS Code: **Commit** in the commit box of the Changes view, and `Ctrl+Enter` (`Cmd+Enter`) in its message, send `feature/run` of `commit.create` to Gitardo's engine with the message typed as the answer `message` (`engineRuns` in, run by). The question when nothing is staged is a modal warning dialog with **Stage all and commit** (`VscodeAsker` in), and the plan is shown in a modal dialog when `gitardo.commandPreview` is `all`; once it ran, the engine's follow-up `emptyCommitMessage` empties the commit box (the message `committed`). The message the box starts from comes from the engine's follow-ups, posted as `commitMessage` by: after a squash merge, `preparedMessage`, for which the extension reads what `.git/SQUASH_MSG` says (`reads.squashMessage`, `commit.prepared-message`); after a split, `fillCommitMessage`, with the message of the commit split. When the engine's binary is missing, it says so.
@card extension commit.amend
title: Amend the last commit
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Replaces the last commit with one that has the staged changes too, and the new message if one is written.
In VS Code: ticking **Amend last commit** in the commit box of the Changes view, then **Amend** or `Ctrl+Enter`, sends `feature/run` of `commit.amend` to Gitardo's engine with the message typed, empty to keep the one the commit has, as the answer `message` (`engineRuns` in, run by); the plan is shown in a modal dialog when `gitardo.commandPreview` is `all` (`VscodeAsker`). When the engine's binary is missing, it says so.
@card extension commit.options
title: The options of a commit
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
Signs the next commit or not, adds `Signed-off-by` and co-authors to its message, or skips the hooks, for that commit, from a menu beside **Commit**.
In VS Code: the same button, **Commit options** with the Codicon `settings`, beside **Commit** in the commit box of the **Gitardo Changes** view, its menu drawn by the view (`commitOptionsMenu` in), the line under the box too; the options go to the engine in the answers of `feature/run` of `commit.create` and `commit.amend` (`optionAnswers` in), and the setting is `gitardo.commit.signOff`. As in the window, what an entry signs with or adds is its tooltip. The options of each repository are kept by the view while another is shown. VS Code's own **Commit (Signed Off)** and **Commit (No Verify)** are of its Source Control, not of Gitardo's box.
@card extension commit.option-arguments
title: The arguments of the options of a commit

What the options of the commit box add to `git commit`, for `commit.create` and `commit.amend`.
In VS Code: the engine's, given the options in the answers of `feature/run`; the co-authors are known from its first call, as a flag is, since no question asks them (protocol 1.13).

@card extension commit.prepared-message
title: The message git prepared
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
The message git wrote for the commit of a squash merge while its changes wait to be committed, which fills the commit box after `merge.squash`.
In VS Code: the commit box of the Changes view; the extension kept the line breaks at the end of the message.
@card extension commit.draft-message
title: Draft the commit message
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
Asks the AI provider to write the commit message from the changes, and puts its answer in the message box, to edit.
In VS Code: the sparkle button of the commit box of the Changes view, titled *Draft a message from the changes with the language model set up in VS Code*. in asks the engine `read/commit.draft-message` with the subjects of the graph: the engine reads the changes and the diff (`commit.read-draft-diff`), writes the request and cleans the answer, and asks VS Code's language models in place of the AI provider with `host/languageModel`, which `answerLanguageModel` in sends to the first model gives, with no effort, and with the justification *Gitardo sends your uncommitted changes to the model to draft a commit message.* (G3: not the model of `gitardo.conflicts.aiModel`). The engine drafts it; the extension only sends the request the engine wrote to VS Code's model and gives the answer back. When the engine's binary is missing, it says so. The status bar says *Gitardo: drafting commit message* while it runs. With no model, it refuses with *Gitardo: No language model is available in VS Code. Install an extension that provides one, such as GitHub Copilot.*
@card extension commit.read-draft-diff
title: The diff a draft is written from
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
The diff `commit.draft-message` sends to the model: of what is staged when something is, as **Commit** would commit it, and of every change of the tracked files otherwise.
In VS Code: the engine reads it for `read/commit.draft-message`; the extension does not read it.
@card extension identity.read
title: Read the identity
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
Who commits will be made as in this repository, and which of the `user.*` keys the repository sets for itself.
@card extension identity.switch
title: Switch identity
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: quick search
Sets the name and email that commits are made with in this repository, from a profile of the settings or typed.
In VS Code: **Gitardo: Switch Identity** in the Command Palette, **Switch identity…** in the **…** menu of the toolbar of the graph, and a click on the identity under the commit box of the Changes view. Each sends `feature/run` of `identity.switch` to Gitardo's engine (in); the pick is a quick pick, and the name and email input boxes. **Edit the list of identities…** is added to the pick by the extension, which opens the settings at `gitardo.identities` and runs nothing (`HostHooks.choices`, B9). When the engine's binary is missing, it says so.
@card extension identity.use-global
title: Use the global identity again
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Removes the identity the repository set for itself, so that the one of the global configuration applies again.
Entry points: **Use the global identity**, described **Remove this repository's own setting**, in the pick of `identity.switch`.
Offered when: the repository has at least one of `user.name`, `user.email`, `user.signingkey` of its own.
Refuses: "This repository already uses the global identity." when it has none of the three.
@card extension changes.ignore-patterns
title: The patterns that ignore a file
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
The patterns offered to ignore a file or a folder, the files each can be written in, and the line written there.
In VS Code: worked out by the engine for `changes.ignore`; the extension shows the patterns and the places in VS Code's quick picks.
@card extension changes.ignore
title: Ignore a file or a folder
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Has git ignore a file or a folder, by a pattern written where the user chooses; on a file git tracks, offers to stop tracking it too, since a pattern does not stop git from following a file it tracks.
In VS Code: **Ignore…** in the context menu of a file of the **Gitardo Changes** view (`changes.row-menu`) and of a file or a folder of VS Code's Explorer (`gitardo.ignore` in `explorer/context`, under **Link into New Worktrees**, not on the root folder of a repository), sending `feature/run` of `changes.ignore` to Gitardo's engine with the path from the top of the repository, its questions asked in VS Code's pickers (`VscodeAsker`). From the Explorer the views show the repository of the file first. The Explorer cannot know what git ignores, so it offers **Ignore…** on a file ignored already, which the engine refuses saying by which pattern. No **Open `<file>`** after it, as in the desktop. VS Code's own **Add to.gitignore** of its Source Control view stays as it is.
@card extension changes.stop-tracking
title: Stop tracking a file, and keep it on disk
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Takes a file, or every file of a folder, out of the index, so that the next commit deletes it from the repository while it stays on disk: for a file committed by mistake, such as a file of local settings, once it is ignored.
In VS Code: **Stop tracking, keep on disk…** in the context menu of a tracked file of the **Gitardo Changes** view, and **Stop Tracking, Keep on Disk…** in that of a file or a folder of VS Code's Explorer (`gitardo.stopTracking`), which does not know which files git tracks and leaves the engine to refuse, sending `feature/run` of `changes.stop-tracking` to the engine, its danger asked in a modal dialog (`VscodeAsker`).
@card extension changes.read-skipped
title: Read the files hidden from git
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
Which tracked files git is told not to look at, and how: those marked **skip-worktree**, whose changes stay out of commits and which a checkout leaves alone, and those marked **assume unchanged**, a promise git may forget.
In VS Code: read by the engine with the state of the repository; the extension shows what it reads.
@card extension changes.skip
title: Hide a file's changes from git
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Has git leave the changes of a tracked file alone: they stay on disk and out of every commit, as for a settings file that each one changes on their own computer.
In VS Code: **Hide its changes from git…** in the context menu of a tracked file of the **Gitardo Changes** view, and **Hide Its Changes from Git…** in that of a file of VS Code's Explorer (`gitardo.hideFromGit`), not of a folder nor of a file hidden already, sending `feature/run` of `changes.skip` to the engine; on a file git does not track the engine refuses with **Ignore…**.
@card extension changes.unskip
title: Show a file's changes to git again
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: show where
Takes back what `changes.skip` did, for one file or for every file hidden.
In VS Code: the same entries in the group **Hidden from git** of the **Gitardo Changes** view, the one of a file also as the eye button of its row, and **Show Its Changes to Git Again** in the context menu of VS Code's Explorer on a file hidden from git (`gitardo.showToGit`), sending `feature/run` of `changes.unskip` to the engine. The Explorer knows the hidden files of the repository the views show only (the context key `gitardo.hiddenFromGit`), so that a file hidden in another repository of the workspace offers **Hide Its Changes from Git…**, which the engine refuses as hidden already.
@card extension changes.skipped-list
title: The files hidden from git, under the changes
place: the draft and the Changes page
show: none
The files git is told not to look at, listed under the changes while there are any, so that they are not forgotten.
In VS Code: a group **Hidden from git** at the end of the **Gitardo Changes** view (`HiddenGroup` in), from `skipped` in the state the engine sends (protocol 1.11): its name and count fold it, its **…** has **Show all to git again**, and each row has the icon and the name of the file, its folder, **skip-worktree** or **assumed unchanged**, the same tooltip after its path, the eye button of **Show its changes to git again**, and the same menu (`hiddenFileMenu` in). A click opens VS Code's diff between `HEAD` and the file on disk. No snapshot: the extension's views have none, and the UI runs are left to the pipeline.
@card extension branch.checkout
title: Check out a branch
place: the branch rail
show: show where
Switches the working tree to a local branch.
In VS Code: **Check out `<branch>`** in the context menu of a local branch in the references tree, the Branches view and on its label in the graph; a double click there and in **Recent** and **Favorites** (`branch.open`). Each sends `feature/run` of `branch.checkout` to Gitardo's engine (in, `engineRuns` in), with the branch as its target; the plan is shown in a modal dialog when it asks first or `gitardo.commandPreview` is `all` (`VscodeAsker` in). The settings are `gitardo.submodules.updateOnCheckout` and `gitardo.commandPreview`. When the engine's binary is missing, it says so.
@card extension branch.open
title: What a double click on a branch does
place: the branch rail
show: none
Decides what a double click on a local or a remote branch does, wherever it is listed or labels its commit, and which entry comes first in its context menu.
In VS Code: the same choice, made in `openBranch` and `openRemoteBranch` of for a double click in the references tree, the Branches view and on a label of the graph, and in the menus, which offer the entries of the other worktree in place of **Check out**; `gitardo.branches.openIn` is the setting. The checkout it leads to is sent to the engine as its card says; opening a worktree stays the extension's.
@card extension branch.checkout-remote

place: the branch rail
show: show where
Checks out a remote branch in the local branch of its name: a new one that tracks it, or the one there is, brought up to it.
In VS Code: in the context menu of a remote branch, **Check out as local branch** when no local branch has its name and **Check out `<branch>`** when one has; a double click on it in the references tree, the Branches view or on its label in the graph (`branch.open`). Each sends `feature/run` of `branch.checkout-remote` to Gitardo's engine (in, `engineRuns` in), with the remote branch as its target; its question is a quick pick. The menu and the double click open the worktree that has the local branch instead, so the engine's refusal is met only by a caller that goes past them (B6). When the engine's binary is missing, it says so.

@card extension branch.pane
title: The branch rail, its buttons and its comparison
place: the branch rail
show: none
The branch rail as one place, the legend and the filter of the timeline: its buttons, in the heading of **Branches** of the side column; the recent branches and the favorites, the local branches, the branches of each remote under a heading of its own, and the stashes, worktrees, tags and submodules kept aside, with their context menus (`refs.tree`); and the comparison of a reference with the branch checked out.
